The field now earns the refusal the same type earns in a signature, from the same classification rather than a second copy of it, so the two positions cannot drift apart about a type that classification covers — a function type is not one of them, since the layout refuses it at its own arm, and the two positions do render it differently. The field's declaration is looked for in the scope that **declares the struct** — its file, and the `spec` block inside it — because a struct declared inside a `spec` block is not among its file's top-level definitions, and reading the access site's scope would find no declaration at all and leave the accusation standing. Two consequences travelled with it. A field access on a struct reached only through an array parameter — `pub fn h(v: [S; 2]) -> i32 { return v[0].g; }` — is the first thing that asks such a struct for a layout, and asserted the layout had succeeded, so that program **aborted the compiler with exit 101** through the ordinary CLI with analysis running; it now exits 1 with the diagnostic. And a struct carrying such a field was read by A026 and A027 as containing a *compound* field, so a program with one supported level of nesting was rejected for nesting it does not have, ahead of the refusal that names the real reason — a field's unresolved name is now compound only when it names a struct, which is what `has_compound_fields` beside it already did. Migration, in both directions: every shape that reached a *layout* was already a refusal or an abort, so nothing that compiled before stops compiling; but a declaration nothing lays out — an unused struct with such a field — was rejected by A026 or A027 for the wrong reason and now compiles, because no phase computes its layout and no rule owns the carrier. A051's own suite asserts the opposite policy for its carrier, that an unused struct with a field the layout has no size for must not compile, so the two disagree until a declaration-anchored rule owns the `spec` name; the gap is asserted in `tests/src/codegen/wasm/negative.rs` so that closing it is a deliberate edit ([#320])
- New fatal codegen diagnostic **`CodegenError::NameSectionSymbolCollision`** (proof mode only): two of a program's own functions rendering one WASM `name`-section symbol is now rejected once a proof obligation *applies* that symbol. The file-qualification the [#362] name-section split introduces can still make a struct method and a free function collide — a struct `lib` with `fn helper()` in the entry file and a free `fn helper()` in `lib.inf` both render `lib.helper` — and nothing downstream can tell a `T_app`/`HA_app_ok` over the shared symbol which one it names. The message names both definitions as written (`method 'helper' on struct 'lib'`, `function 'helper' in file 'lib'`). A shared symbol no obligation applies still builds — the two functions still receive distinct `Definition` names, appending the WASM function index to the second — and compile mode is unaffected either way, since it carries no obligations to misresolve ([#362])
- Universal (`forall`/plain) specification bodies now lower to vanilla WebAssembly, so **a proof-mode `.wasm` loads under standard tooling**: across the fixture corpus, proof artifacts passing stock `wasm-tools validate` go from 5 of 29 to 29 of 29. A `forall` body previously kept its `0xfc` quantifier wrapper and drew each `@` through a custom uzumaki opcode, so reading the proof artifact required the `inf-wasmparser` fork; `exists`/`unique` bodies already lowered this way, because their reachability obligation reduces the retained body under the plain semantics where a wrapper has no rule. A spec function's `@`s now arrive as hidden trailing parameters in every covered mode, planned by a new `choice` pass that is a pure code-generation concept — no notion of obligations or quantifiers — of which reachability becomes one client, keeping its own frame contract and no-return rule. This changes what the artifact loads as, not what it proves: a `forall` spec function stays omitted from the module record (omission keys on the obligation kind, never on the bytes) and the quantifier is carried by the obligation, which is built from the typed AST. **Fixes a live hole**: spec methods were passed no plan at all, so a spec method with a nondeterministic block and an `@` compiled with custom opcodes and was invisible to every check — the planner now iterates the same buckets code generation compiles, so its scope cannot drift again. The choice suffix starts at the parameter slot *observed* at the suffix site rather than at the source arity, because a receiver and a compound return's `sret` pointer occupy leading slots without being declared parameters, and both are `i32`, so a misaligned load would read `self` and still validate. A choice run is discriminated on its planned kind and never on how many leaves it has: a one-element array is a one-leaf aggregate, indistinguishable from a scalar by count, and a count test would skip the frame-slot store and bind a raw value where later accesses expect a frame pointer — an error that validates cleanly and trips no assertion. The crate writes a `0xfc` byte at exactly two sites; each bumps a counter and every choice-lowered body asserts both are zero before it is sealed, as a real assertion rather than a debug one, which would vanish from release builds and never covered the block wrappers at all. New `CodegenError::ChoiceSuffixTooLarge` names the offending specification function instead of letting the emitted module fail its own verification step as a malformed binary — the one shape this can newly reject, and pathological. Unaffected: compile-mode output is byte-identical, every compile-mode golden included, and every obligation payload and theorem in the emitted `.v` is byte-identical; only the `mod_types` entry of a universal spec function that draws an `@` changes, and only in content — entry counts and positions are unchanged, so no surviving function's `modfunc_type` moves ([#364])
- Dynamic array indices are guarded in **every** build, proof mode included, so the artifact a proof is written about is byte-for-byte the artifact that ships. Proof mode used to be the one unguarded path: a dynamic `arr[i]` lowered to a bare `BI_load`/`BI_store` carrying no side condition, leaving the emitted `.v` weaker than the deployed module on exactly the property the language exists to establish. The guard itself is unchanged — `index >=u length -> unreachable` at the shared `emit_index_offset` choke point, a single unsigned comparison, so a negative index arrives as a huge `u32` and traps — and the `emit_bounds_checks` toggle stays because `Compiler` is a library type whose in-crate tests exercise both settings — not as a hook for [#215], whose elision is decided per *access* inside `emit_index_offset` and leaves the toggle `true` for every module. The reservation scan that decides whether a function needs the guard's scratch local now asks the same folding function emission asks rather than restating the condition as "the index is not a number literal": the two already disagreed on a literal index whose `index * element_size` overflows (`arr[1073741824]` on an `[i64; 4]`), which the scan called constant and reserved nothing for while emission took the dynamic branch and reached the guard. Compile mode already panicked on it; the proof path was the half that returned before the guard, and now does not. It was latent on the CLI path because analysis rule A037 rejects such an index before code generation runs, so only an analysis-skipping entry point could reach it. Unaffected: compile-mode output is byte-identical, as is proof-mode output for any module with no dynamic array index; every committed `.wasm`/`.wat` golden is unchanged ([#214])
- `core/wasm-codegen`'s by-reference gate now elides a compound parameter's entry copy when every linked `external fn` it reaches declares **no** `mut` parameter at all, so a pure-math library no longer costs its callers a copy. A parameter that reaches an external declaring *any* `mut` parameter still keeps its copy for **every** position it is handed at, not only the `mut` one — the gate decides per external, not per parameter, because narrowing further would rest on a containment property the linker's Tier-B provenance proof does not establish, so `sort_pair(mut a, b)` still costs `b` a copy, recorded as a deliberate limitation lifted only once [#420]'s containment analysis exists. Unaffected: a program with no linked externals, or one whose externals are all wholly writing, is byte-identical ([#333])
- The linear memory layout is now a value on `CodegenOptions` rather than two constants: `MemoryLayout { pages, stack_size }` is the single source for the memory section (`minimum == maximum`, so the memory is fixed rather than growable), the `__stack_pointer` initializer, and the per-frame size assertion. `validate` rejects a layout that cannot describe a real memory, including one whose memory and stack together span more than the 32-bit address space — the headroom a wrapped stack pointer needs to land out of bounds and trap. No user-facing knob yet; the default (one page, all stack) leaves every golden artifact byte-identical ([#363])

- Compound (struct/array) parameters the callee provably never writes are now passed by reference — no frame slot, entry copy, or (when nothing else needs memory) frame at all; a copy remains only for written parameters or those passed to an `external fn`, recovering part of the size growth from [#315] ([#220])
- An immutable `self` receiver forwarded to a compound-parameter `external fn` now copies into the method's frame on entry, so foreign writes no longer mutate the caller's struct through an immutable receiver; a checked write-set contract on `external fn` parameters ships in [#333], which also withdraws the ability to forward an immutable receiver to a `mut`-declared external position at all ([#329])
- Bulk-memory-free output by default: codegen emits no `memory.fill`/`memory.copy` unless a build opts in, so Compile-mode modules are plain WebAssembly 1.0 (+ mutable-globals); frame zero-fill and compound copies lower to inline stores/loops, and artifacts that carried no bulk op stay byte-identical ([#315])
- Opt-in WebAssembly feature selection: `wasm-features = ["bulk-memory"]` under `[build]` in `Inference.toml` (or `infc --wasm-features bulk-memory`) restores the single-instruction bulk forms byte-identically; the shared vocabulary lives in `core/compiler-interface` (ABI minor 1 → 2) ([#315])
- Multi-file codegen: flatten the import-reachable file closure into one WASM module; the file-qualified `FnKey` (new `inference-fn-key` crate) keeps same-named functions distinct, structs lay out per defining file, only entry-file `pub fn`s export, and single-file output stays byte-identical ([#63])
- Fixed: multi-dimensional scalar array literal initialization (`[[i32; 3]; 2]`) no longer panics — new recursive `store_array_literal_elements` stores each scalar leaf at its computed offset; single-dimensional output is byte-identical
- Fixed: nested array-of-structs literal initialization (`[[Pt; 2]; 2]`) no longer panics — `store_array_literal_elements` gains a struct-leaf arm reusing the single-dimensional AoS machinery; single-dimensional AoS output is byte-identical
- Runtime bounds checks for dynamic array indices: `emit_index_offset` guards reads and writes with an unsigned length compare trapping via `unreachable` in all Compile-mode builds; the proof-mode half is [#214], which lands in this same release and makes the guard unconditional ([#164])
- `FunctionOrigin { TopLevel, SpecInner }` threaded through `visit_function_definition`; spec-inner functions can no longer be WASM-exported even when `pub` ([issue#19])
- Per-spec function-index map (`spec_func_indices_by_spec`) replaces the single union list; spec-inner functions key as `"<SpecName>.<fn>"` so two specs may share function names, `name` section stays unmangled ([issue#21])
- Emit `inference.spec_funcs` WASM custom section in `proof` mode carrying the per-spec index map, making bare `.wasm` binaries self-describing; omitted in `compile` mode so binaries stay byte-identical ([issue#16])
- wasm-to-v: new `errors.rs` with `WasmToVError` thiserror enum (`InvalidRocqIdentifier`, `RocqStdlibShadow`, `EmbeddedSpecMismatch`, `WasmParse`) and `InvalidIdentifierReason` sub-enum ([issue#20])
- wasm-to-v: `validate_rocq_identifier` rejects Rocq-illegal module/spec names (bad leading char, invalid chars, length > 255, stdlib shadow, reserved keyword) before `Definition <name>` emission ([issue#20])
- wasm-to-v: per-spec Rocq emission — one `Definition <mod>__<SpecName>_hspec{k}` per obligation plus a gathering `_specs` list and a `Theorem valid_<mod>__<SpecName> : ValidSpec …` per spec; obligation-free specs render `(@nil hassert)` ([issue#21], [issue#22])
- Switch from LLVM to direct WebAssembly emission via `wasm-encoder`: all LLVM dependencies removed (`inkwell`, `inf-llc`, `rust-lld`), non-det instructions emitted as custom `0xfc`-prefix opcodes, all `pub` functions exported (reactor model, no `_start`) ([#125])
- Add compilation architecture with `CodegenOutput` boundary: `codegen()` returns WASM bytes plus metadata, with new `Target` (Wasm32/Soroban), `CompilationMode` (Compile/Proof), and `OptLevel` (O0–O3/Os/Oz) enums ([issue#97], [#125])
- Add per-function optimization strategy for proof mode (Decision #32): spec functions compile unoptimized to preserve structural correspondence for Rocq, execution functions use release optimization; `OptLevel` is metadata only for now ([issue#97])
- Add validation guards in `codegen()`: reject proof mode with non-Wasm32 targets, reject Soroban with non-det operations ([issue#97])
- Upgrade shadowing detection from `debug_assert!` to `assert!` in `pre_scan_locals` — fires in release builds for parameter, constant, and variable name collisions in `locals_map`
- Add `Statement::Loop` body recursion to `pre_scan_locals()` — locals inside loop bodies are pre-registered before instruction emission
- Add loop and break statement lowering: `loop` emits `block`+`loop` with `br_if` exit and `br 0` back-edge, `break` emits `br <depth>`, and `LoopContext` tracks block depth across loop/if/non-det nesting ([#152])
- Replace silent `if let ArgumentType::Argument` skip with exhaustive `match` covering `SelfReference`, `IgnoreArgument`, and `Type` variants, each with an explicit `todo!()`
- Add fixed-size array support with linear memory allocation: `__stack_pointer` shadow stack with stack-first downward-growing layout, literal/index/uzumaki lowering, copy-on-entry value semantics, sret array returns, 16-byte-aligned zero-filled frames ([#148])
- Add struct type support with linear memory allocation: C-style field layout with natural alignment, literal and member-access lowering, copy-on-entry parameters, sret returns, `memory.copy` value-semantics copies, struct uzumaki ([pr#159])
- Add struct method codegen: methods compile as top-level WASM functions with mangled `TypeName.method_name` names, `self` as an i32 pointer (immutable `self` zero-copy, `mut self` copy-on-entry), instance and associated calls unified via `ResolvedCallee` ([pr#178])
- Add enum type codegen: unit variants lower as i32 constants with declaration-order zero-based tags, usable in all value positions, with `==`/`!=` comparisons and uzumaki support ([pr#187])
- Add nested compound type codegen (struct-in-struct, array-in-struct, struct-in-array): recursive `type_byte_size()`, `CompoundFieldLayout` sub-layout caching, pointer-chained access loading at the terminal scalar; one nesting level enforced by analysis rule A026 ([pr#185])
- Add per-element zero-store elision in array and struct literal codegen: stores of zero-valued elements are skipped during frame-local initialization — the prologue `memory.fill 0` already zeroed the frame ([#188])
- Eliminate dead trailing epilogue in non-void functions — each `return` emits its own epilogue and analysis rule A007 guarantees a return on every path, shrinking every non-void function with a frame ([#188])
- Add assignment statement lowering: `mut` support in AST and type-checker (`AssignToImmutable`), identifier and array-index targets, mutable function parameters, and literal type propagation in assignments ([#146])
- Add conditional statement lowering: `if`/`else` lowers to WASM structured control flow, `pre_scan_locals` recurses into both arms, and non-void functions get a trailing `unreachable` safety net ([#144])
- Add binary and unary expression lowering: all arithmetic, comparison, logical, bitwise, and shift operators for i32/i64 with sign-sensitive dispatch; `-x` via `0 - x`, `!x` via `i32.eqz`, `~x` via `x ^ -1`; `**` deferred ([#140])
- Add function parameter lowering and function call support: parameters map to WASM locals `0..n`, a pre-scan `func_name_to_idx` map enables forward references, and expression-statement calls `Drop` unused return values ([#136])
- Add local variable lowering (`let` bindings): `local.set`/`local.get` for literal, identifier, and uzumaki initializers across all numeric types and bool; `ConstantDefinition` now shares the `lower_literal` helper ([pr#135])
- Add LLVM-based WASM code generation using `inf-llc` ([#44])
- Add custom LLVM intrinsics for non-deterministic instructions ([#44])
- Implement `forall`, `exists`, `uzumaki`, `assume`, `unique` block codegen ([#44])
- Add `rust-lld` linker invocation for WASM linking ([#44])
- Add mutable globals support in WASM compilation ([#44])
- Add base WASM code generation from typed AST ([#29])

### Analysis

- New analysis rule **A052** (`ConstantArithmeticOverflow`, error) rejects an operation whose operands fold to constants and whose result leaves the type it is performed at. `+`, `-`, `*` and unary `-` trap on such a result, so the operation is not a value the program computes but a trap it takes on every run that reaches it, and no `assume`, envelope or specification recovers a result the type cannot hold — which is why this is an error and not a warning. Literals, the `const` bindings of the same function body, parentheses, arithmetic-mode annotations and nested arithmetic fold; a `let` binding does not, because it names a variable whose value the language does not promise stays put, and neither does a call, which the annotation does not reach into either. An operator inside a `wrapping(...)` is exempt and its region folds **modularly** rather than not at all: `wrapping(2147483647 + 1)` is now the only way to write a constant that wraps, so the fold looks through the annotation and hands the wrapped value to whatever encloses it, while a checked operation whose result leaves the type folds to nothing — which is also what makes a chain of operators report its innermost overflow once instead of at every level above it. A022 keeps a literal that does not fit the type it is measured at, and this rule folds nothing containing one, so `let x: u8 = 300 + 1;` stays one finding about one literal; the handoff reads one shared predicate rather than restating the comparison, the convention A044 and A046 already use, and it is what lets the message tell a reader that both operands fit the type and the operation does not. The expression is rendered from the arena — `const max: i32 = 2147483647; return max + 1;` is reported as `max + 1`, with `2147483647` and `1` given as the folded operand values — because rendering only the folded form shows an expression nobody wrote, and the wrapped result appears only in the remedy, since under a default that traps saying the type "holds" it would be false. A `forall`-quantified specification body and an unquantified one are skipped, because the translator turns each into an obligation term and lowers neither, in either compilation mode — there is no run for the operation to trap on, and a term's `+` is the machine's modular operator; an `exists`/`unique` body is compiled and reduced, so it is examined like any other, as is a function outside the `spec` block that a specification merely calls. The overflow this work opened with, a fixed-point multiply whose product leaves `i64`, is deliberately not one of its findings: those operands are parameters, nothing folds, and that overflow belongs to the emitted guard at run time ([#316])
- New analysis rule **A053** (`ArithModeGovernsNothing`, error) requires a `checked(...)` or `wrapping(...)` to contain an operator it can govern. The annotation reaches the `+`, `-`, `*` and unary `-` written between its own parentheses and nothing else, and two shapes account for nearly every instance where it reaches none: a call, whose body the annotation does not enter — `wrapping(mix(s))` is the mistake authors of hashes, mixers and pseudo-random generators make most often — and a glued negative literal such as `-2147483648`, which is one token carrying its own sign rather than a negation applied to a value. Both are named in the message, because neither is visible from the site the rule fires on. Two wordings: a `wrapping(...)` that governs nothing is inert, while a `checked(...)` that governs nothing reads as a guarantee no guard backs, and a later reader would be wrong to believe it. An error rather than a warning, because this is a typo — the annotation is somewhere other than where it was meant to go, and no default makes it right ([#316])
- New analysis rule **A054** (`ArithModeChangesNothing`, warning) reports an annotation naming the mode already in force where it is written. A warning and not an error on purpose: which spelling is redundant is decided by the mode unannotated arithmetic has, so the same source is redundant under one default and meaningful under the other, and turning it into a hard error would break source the previous release taught people to write with no deprecation window — the position rustc takes on `unused_attributes`, and one languages whose opt-out is an operator (`+%`, `&+`) never face. The comparison is against the *enclosing* effective mode rather than the default alone, so `checked(a * wrapping(b + c))` is redundant nowhere: the outer annotation changes the `*` and the inner restores wrapping inside the region the outer made checked. A stack of same-mode annotations reports its outermost member once, the convention A048 and A049 already use for nested array annotations, and an annotation A053 owns is skipped through the containment predicate both rules read ([#316])
- Both rules and code generation's guard classifier now read one definition of which operators an arithmetic-mode annotation governs, `GuardedOp` in `inference-ast`, so what a rule calls a governed operator and what the emitter puts a guard on cannot drift apart; the language's default mode is likewise one value, `ArithMode::DEFAULT`, that the analysis rules, the proof-mode diagnostics and the emitter all read ([#316])
- New analysis rule **A048** (`StringNotSupported`, error) rejects every position at which a `string` value could be introduced: a string literal in any expression position, and `string` or `String` as the recorded type of a `let` or a `const` at any scope, as the type of a function, method or `external fn` parameter or return, or as the type of a struct field. Every type position looks through array nesting at any depth, so `[[string; 2]; 3]` is one finding on the annotation that carries it rather than one per layer, and reporting is per offending construct rather than per declaration, so `let s: string = "hi";` reports twice — the annotation and the literal are two separate things to remove. The two names stay registered as root-scope builtin type names, so every such annotation still type-checks, and nothing after the type checker could act on one: there is no layout for a string in linear memory, no WebAssembly value type to pass one in, and no term for a proof to describe one with. That produced three unrelated failures for one missing feature — an abort on the literal, an abort one layer earlier in the byte-size computation that lays out a frame or a struct, and a clean unsupported-type error on a signature — none of them a diagnostic anyone could act on. The message names the position that offended and the modelling that does have a layout: a `[u8; N]` with its bytes written as numbers, or an enum tag when the value is one of a fixed set. The rule descends into `spec` bodies, which proof mode lowers to real WebAssembly functions, and reports there in either mode; where the proof translation also rejects the program it is reported twice, since the crate has no cross-rule suppression ([#360])
- New analysis rule **A049** (`UnitAsValue`, error) draws the line between the *absence* of a value and a *value of nothing*. The first is the point of the unit type and is implemented — a function whose return type is `()` or omitted returns nothing, and code generation gives it an empty result list — so the return type is deliberately not covered. The second is a declaration that cannot be honoured, and the rule rejects it as the recorded type of a `let` or a `const`, as the type of a function, method or `external fn` parameter, and as the type of a struct field, looking through array nesting at any depth: a unit value carries no information, so it occupies no bytes and has no WebAssembly type, and a parameter declared `()` is given no argument slot to arrive in, a binding of it has nothing to store, an array of it has no element size for frame layout to compute, and a struct field of it has no offset that means anything. The type has one spelling, `()`. A unit *literal* is rejected everywhere except as the whole expression of a `return` or of an expression statement, after peeling parentheses — the parser synthesizes a unit literal for the missing expression of a bare `return`, so rejecting the literal unconditionally would reject every void function ever written, and stating the exemption on the root keeps `return;`, `return ();`, `();` and `(());` from differing by a pair of brackets. The exemption reaches the root and nothing below it, so `f(())` is still reported: an argument has to arrive somewhere. The rule generalizes a judgement the linker already made on the extern path alone, where lowering an `external fn` signature failed for a parameter whose value type came back empty; that check stays in place as defence in depth, which is why "has no value representation" is the phrase both use. `spec` bodies are covered for A048's reason ([#360])
- New analysis rule **A050** (`UnnamedParameter`, error) requires a parameter of a function with a body to be written `name: T` or `_: T`, rejecting the bare positional `T` the grammar also admits. On a defined function the bare form occupies the same slot as `_: T` and says strictly less: it binds no name, so the body cannot read the value; it labels nothing, so a call site cannot name it; and where `_: T` is a deliberate declaration that the parameter is present and unused, a bare `T` states nothing at all, leaving two spellings for one thing. It is also the grammar's fallback arm, which is what makes a forgotten type annotation misparse — `fn f(x)` is a parameter whose *type* is `x`, not a parameter named `x`. The check is a declaration walk over free functions, struct methods, `spec` functions and methods of a struct declared inside a `spec`, with no bodies read and no type resolution beyond rendering the type for the message; the index counts declared parameters from zero with a `self` receiver excluded, so `fn m(self, i32)` reports parameter 0 — the numbering the type checker already uses when it talks about an argument of that method — and a method renders as `Struct::method`. `external fn` is a documented non-scope: an extern declares an ABI signature with no body to read a parameter in, so a positional type is a complete statement of it, and it is the spelling the corpus uses. It is nonetheless the wrong form on an extern whose linked body *writes* through a parameter, since `mut` is a field of a named parameter alone and an unnamed one cannot carry the write-set contract A047 checks — a recommendation the rule does not enforce, because a read-only extern is a fine use of the bare form. Unlike A048 and A049 this rule is not a gate on an unimplemented feature and does not become obsolete when some later feature lands: `_: T` is implemented, and the bare form is rejected because one spelling for a concept is better than two. Removing the weaker of two spellings is the direction A033 and A046 already took. `all_rules()` now registers 47 rules ([#360])
- New analysis rule **A051** (`GenericNotSupported`, error) refuses generic code before code generation, in both of the forms the grammar admits: a `fn` declaring type parameters (`fn id T'(x: T) -> T`) and a type application (`Q i32'`) written as a type or standing in expression position. The type checker handles generics in full — at each call site it infers a type argument from the arguments it was given and checks the body against it — and nothing carries that substitution any further, so a type parameter arrives at code generation still standing for no type at all: no layout to size a frame slot with, no WebAssembly value type to pass a value in, and no term for a proof to describe one with. A type *argument* is worse off still, because no type declaration in the language accepts one — a struct, an enum and an `external fn` each declare a bare name, and only a function binds a type parameter — so `Q i32'` names no declaration the compiler could lay out. What that produced before the rule was three outcomes, and only the first of them was a failure: a binder appearing in a lowered type reached an unsupported-type refusal carrying no source location, because signature lowering is handed one type node at a time with no enclosing binder list and so cannot tell `T` from a misspelled type name; a binder appearing nowhere lowered was emitted and exported anyway; and a binder shadowing a declared type was read one way by the type checker and the other by code generation, which is a miscompile rather than a missing diagnostic. Code generation now backstops each of those routes instead of emitting: a declaration's type parameters are refused at the head of the function-body visitor, ahead of the signature it would otherwise lower; a type application in a signature, or below an array in one, is refused by signature lowering; and a value of a generic type is refused by the fallible layout wrappers — each naming A051 as the rule that rejects the program first, alongside the expression-position refusal the poison slot already carried, and each reachable only from a caller that skips analysis. The declaration predicate is the declared binders and nothing else, with no reachability filter and no entry-point carve-out, since each candidate narrowing leaves one of those three standing. Every type position looks through array nesting at any depth, so `[[Q i32'; 2]; 3]` is one finding on the annotation that carries it rather than one per layer, and the expression half is a recursive descent rather than a pass over statement roots, because `(Q i32');` puts the type inside a parenthesization. Reporting anchors on declarations and annotations and never on call sites — an uncalled generic reaches the same dead end, so the defect belongs to the declaration — and where one declaration is both, as `fn g T'(p: Q T')` is, the binder and the application are reported separately, because they are two different edits. The rendered spelling is read from the syntax rather than from the type the checker recorded, whose rendering puts a tick on the base as well (`Q' i32'`), a spelling the grammar does not admit. Function types are refused for their own reason, with or without a type parameter in them, and this rule does not claim them. The rule descends into `spec` bodies and reports there in either mode, for A048's reason. Only the declaration half is a gate on an unimplemented feature and is deleted the day monomorphization lands ([#76]); the type-application and expression-position halves state a property of the language and survive that day. `all_rules()` now registers 48 rules ([#320])
- **A024**'s message no longer claims a call to an unbound `external fn` "is not supported in codegen" because "external functions cannot be compiled to WebAssembly yet" — false since [#9] gave a *bound* extern a body through the static merge, and doubly so since [#363] let a specification apply that body. It now says what is actually missing (no `use … from` directive binds the declaration) and names the fix, including the spec-inner case where the declaration must be moved out of its `spec` block before it can be bound. The check itself is unchanged — only a call through an unbound declaration is rejected, resolved through the same scope-aware `ExternIndex` walk as before ([#362])
- New analysis rule **A047** (`ExternMutArgument`, error) requires a struct or array argument at a `mut` `external fn` parameter to be rooted at a `mut` binding — the call-site half of the write-set contract described above. It resolves the callee through the same scope-aware `ExternIndex` walk A024 uses, since an `external fn` may be declared at file scope or inside a `spec` and the two may share a name, and it is dormant for existing code until some declaration actually marks a parameter `mut`. `all_rules()` now registers 44 rules ([#333])
- A036's shadow-stack budget now travels in a new `AnalysisOptions`, threaded through `Rule::check` to every rule, replacing a `STACK_BUDGET_BYTES` constant that had to be hand-synced with codegen's stack size and could not be checked from either crate. `analyze` keeps its signature and means "default layout"; `analyze_with_options` is the entry point a configured build must use. A cross-crate test now asserts the two defaults agree ([#363])

- Whole-program call graph for the module hierarchy, keyed on the structured `FnKey` from `inference-fn-key`: A035 (recursion) and A036 (stack depth) now span files, catching cross-file recursion and >64 KB cross-file stack chains ([#63])
- Restore the duplicate-`FnKey` debug tripwire in `resolve_adjacency` dropped by the LSP server rewrite ([#239]); it now exempts parse-recovered keys (`is_parse_recovered`), and keep-first behavior is unchanged in every build ([#255])
- Add `core/analysis/` crate with rule-based static analysis between type checking and codegen: five rules (A001–A005), a `Rule` trait with `rule!` macro, a shared AST walker, and a three-severity model ([#156])
- Expand the analysis pass from 5 to 22 rules, migrating 13 checks from the type checker (now type-correctness only): new A006–A011 and A023–A024, migrated A012–A019 and A022
- Add 5 analysis rules for nested compound type constraints: A026 `NestedCompoundDepth`, A027 `UzumakiOnNestedStruct`, A028 `UzumakiOnStructInArray`, A029 `CompoundLiteralMemberAssign`, A031 `UnsupportedCompoundReturnExpr` ([pr#185])
- A033 `CombinedUnaryOperators`: reject adjacent prefix unary operators such as `--x`, `~~x`, `!!x`, and parenthesized variants like `-(~x)` (issues [#82], [#81]; PRs [#111], [#117])
- A035 `RecursionDetected`: reject all direct and mutual/indirect recursion (Power of 10, Rule 1) via a conservative whole-program call graph; recursive codegen fixtures rewritten to iterative form ([#205])
- A036 `StackDepthExceeded`: reject programs whose cumulative shadow-stack usage along a call chain exceeds the 64 KB budget, replacing the opaque runtime `memory.fill` trap with a compile-time error; a parity test keeps the estimate ≥ codegen's real frame size ([#166])
- A037 `ArrayIndexConstOutOfBounds`: reject a constant array index that is negative or `>= length`; dynamic indices remain runtime-guarded ([#164])
- A038 `UzumakiOnCompoundField`: reject uzumaki (@) on a struct- or array-typed struct-literal field (e.g. `Outer { i: @ }`), which slipped past A027 and panicked proof-mode codegen ([#225])
- A039 `StructUzumakiAsArgument`: reject a struct-typed uzumaki (@) passed directly as a function argument (`f(@)`); the array case was already A014, the struct case panicked codegen ([#225])
- A040 `UzumakiOnCompoundArrayElement`: reject a struct- or array-typed uzumaki (@) element of an array literal (scalar `@` elements are supported); distinct from A028's whole-array `@` ([#225])
- A041 `DuplicateLocalName`: reject duplicate function-local names across disjoint sibling blocks with a two-location diagnostic instead of panicking in codegen ([#217])
- A042 `NonDetOutsideSpec`: reject non-deterministic constructs (`forall`/`exists`/`assume`/`unique` blocks, the function-body-modifier form, and via A006 `@`) used lexically outside a `spec { … }` declaration; only the outermost offending block on each path is reported

### AST

- Migrate the AST arena from `FxHashMap<u32, AstNode>` + `Rc<T>` + `RefCell<T>` to typed `Arena<T>` via vendored la-arena: typed indices (`ExprId`, `StmtId`, `DefId`, …) prevent cross-category ID misuse, storage is `Send + Sync` cache-friendly `Vec<T>` ([#156])

### CLI

- **`infc --target stellar` refuses an exported function with a parameter written `_`, a parameter named in more than 30 bytes, or a parameter named what another parameter's `stellar` CLI flag alias spells**, at the source gate in `codegen()` and before any file is written. A caller reaches a contract method's parameter by its name: the contract's `contractspecv0` section records the name in an XDR `string name<30>` field, and `stellar contract invoke` takes each argument as a `--<name>` flag, so a parameter with no name cannot be described and a longer name cannot be recorded whole. Each refusal names the function and the parameter's position — the over-long one also the name, its length and the limit — says what to do: name the parameter, or shorten the name, and ends with the hint to remove `pub` from a function not meant to be a contract method. The unnamed refusal suggests `_unused` for a parameter the body does not read, because a leading underscore is kept as written. A name is taken as the source spells it, so `_amount` is admitted on its own. A name that is another parameter's alias — `pub fn f(x: u32, _x: u32)`, or `x` beside `X`, `x_` or `__x` — is refused, naming both parameters by position and name, which of the two the contested flag belongs to, and saying to rename one of them: `stellar contract invoke` gives each parameter a second flag, its name in kebab case, and resolves a flag to whichever parameter claims it first, in an order that changes from run to run, so a call may reach the wrong parameter or be refused. Names whose aliases merely agree, such as `_x` beside `__x` or `to_addr` beside `toAddr`, keep a flag each and build. The three rules run after the type and return rules, each over every parameter before the next, so `pub fn f(_: u64)` is still refused for its `u64`, the change its author has to make first; the Val-ABI rewriter states the same three rules in the same order, and `tests/tests/stellar_abi_parity.rs` holds the two gates to one answer on an unnamed parameter, on 30- and 31-byte names, on `_amount`, on `x` beside `_x`, `__x`, `X` or `x_` and `amount` beside `Amount`, refused, and on `_x` beside `__x`, `X` beside `_x`, `__` beside `___`, `to_addr` beside `_to_addr` or `toAddr`, `amount` beside `amountX` and a lone `_x`, admitted — naming the same pair when a method's names collide more than once — and to one refusal on a program that breaks two rules at once ([#466])
- **The `-v` + `--target stellar` refusal now names everything the `.v` would lack.** It says the `.v` would describe a module whose exports point at different functions, with none of the value-ABI wrappers and none of the three contract sections the rewrite appends; it said the module had "different exports, different bodies and no environment metadata", while the function bodies are the same and three sections, not one, are missing. This supersedes the `[#324]` `infc -v` bullet below, whose "no metadata section" is left as written per this file's append-and-leave practice. *Migration*: a test or script matching the old clause has to be updated ([#466])
- **`infc --host-imports=<list>` allowlists the host functions a build may bind**, as comma-separated `module.field` pairs — the two strings an embedder registers a function under, joined by a dot. The flag has three states rather than the usual two, which is why it is an `Option<Vec<String>>` where `--wasm-features` beside it is a plain `Vec<String>`: an absent flag applies no policy and every host import the program declares is allowed; `--host-imports=env.clock_ms,fprime_core.command` admits exactly those pairs; and `--host-imports=` — present with an empty value — is a *declared and empty* allowlist that forbids every host import, which is the state a security-minded project sets deliberately and the one an empty vector cannot express, since that is already what an omitted flag produces. The `=` is required, so a bare `--host-imports prog.inf` is a parse error naming the flag rather than a build that read the source path as its policy. Entries are trimmed before they are read, so a quoted paste of the build-log inventory line — which renders its pairs `env.clock_ms, fprime_core.telemetry` — is accepted rather than refused over an invisible leading space (unquoted, the shell splits the list at each space before `infc` sees it); an entry that is empty once trimmed is a stray or trailing comma and gets its own sentence, since quoting an empty entry back would name neither the comma nor the `--host-imports=` spelling it is one character away from. An entry written with the source's own `::` separator gets a sentence of its own too, because neither transcription of `use { clock_ms } from host::env;` is served by the shared one: `env::clock_ms` would be called malformed without being told what is wrong with it, and `host::env.clock_ms` is not malformed under the `module.field` rule at all — it reads as a module named `host::env`, which no declaration can produce, so under the shared rule it would reach the allowlist two phases later and read as a broken mechanism rather than as a typo. That sentence also spells the entry its author meant: a leading `host::` is stripped and what is left is split once, so `fprime_core::telemetry` and `host::fprime_core::telemetry` are both told `fprime_core.telemetry`, and `host::env.clock_ms` — which already uses a dot — is told the prefix is the fault rather than the separator; an entry holding three names gets a generic example instead of a guessed pair. Any other malformed entry is refused before any phase runs, with the whole entry quoted and the `module.field` form spelled out. An import the allowlist does not admit is refused after resolution — and, at `--target spacewasm`, after the registration caps, so the allowlist is never asked to admit a name no embedder can register — and before any file is written, and **every** unadmitted import is reported by one build rather than one per rebuild. The findings are grouped one paragraph per import *module*, naming every unadmitted field of it in one edit, because both mechanisms hold one list per module: two paragraphs asking separately for `env` would ask for a duplicate key in a TOML table and for two halves of one list on a command line. The edit branches on whether that module already has an entry, since the compiler is holding the allowlist rather than asking the reader to look. `infc` is handed a flag and has no manifest, so both branches name the flag as the mechanism — spelled `env.clock_ms`, the only syntax the flag accepts — and the `Inference.toml [host-imports]` table as where `infs` fills it from, with the same edit in the table's own syntax, for the reader who reached the refusal through `infs build` or `infs run` and never typed the flag (see the `### Project Manifest` entry); the empty-allowlist branch spells that edit too, as the key its table with no keys needs written. The source-level half of the remedy names the bindings — "drop every `host::env` binding of `clock_ms`", and "every `host::env` binding of `clock_ms` and `sleep_ms`" for two — rather than quoting a `use` clause back, because the fields it is rendered from are the unadmitted subset of a set deduplicated across the whole program, so a clause spelled from them need not appear in any file; and it says "every" rather than "the" for the same reason, since one deduplicated name can stand for a clause in each of several files, and a reader who drops one is refused again by the next ([#464])
- **`infc` now refuses a proof build that binds a host import, before external resolution runs.** The predicate is whether the build writes a `.v` and not what mode it is in, for the reason `external_spec_policy` already keys on the same flag: `--mode compile -v` is a supported spelling that does write a `.v`, and a gate on the mode alone would let it through with the imports quietly assumed away. It takes both halves of "writes a `.v`" — the request *and* a code-generating phase — so `--analyze --mode proof`, which `normalize_args` has already warned writes no `.v`, still exits 0. The refusal names every host import the program binds and asks for the build without the artifact, naming `-v` and `--mode proof` as the two independent requests for it rather than naming a mode: `-v` sets the flag on its own, so advice to build with `--mode compile` would send `--mode compile -v` round the same refusal having changed nothing. It asks for every request to go, since a build can pass both and a reader who drops one of two is refused again, and it names the one a reader may not have typed: `infs build` passes `--mode proof` itself for a project whose `Inference.toml` sets `[build] mode = "proof"`, and there the setting is what to change. Its "remove the host import(s)" agrees in number with the imports it names. Running it *before* resolution is the load-bearing part: a program that binds both host imports and linked modules is refused by resolution as mixed, and that refusal's first remedy is to bind every extern to `host::…` — advice a proof build must not be given, since following it lands the author at this refusal having rewritten every `use` clause for nothing. Code generation keeps its own proof refusal as the library-level backstop; this one exists to put the governing refusal first ([#464])
- **A `--target spacewasm` build now asks the three SpaceWasm registration caps of the host imports it *declares*, before code generation.** The same rule `spacewasm::check` applies to an artifact's import section, run over `DeclaredImportFacts` built from each resolved declaration, so a 32-byte import module name or a ten-parameter `external fn` is refused while the message can still name the declaration the author wrote rather than a two-level string in a module no file holds. The byte-level check over the linked artifact is unchanged and remains the backstop for an import a linked module dragged in; the two run one body and cannot disagree. The refusal renders through `Violations::render` with the program as its subject, because at that point in the build there is no file to name — and it ends at the source edit. The linked-module alternative an import-name remedy carries is appended by `Violations::render` for the artifact-level check only, behind the same `Checked` discriminant that picks the header, because a name read off a declaration always has an `external fn` behind it and cannot have come from a linked module. `Violation::remedy()` and `Violation::authority()` are crate-private as a result: for an artifact-level finding the remedy is now only part of what its reader is owed, and `render` is the one place the parts are joined. The caps are asked before the allowlist, because an allowlist entry has to spell the import's final name: the other order asks the reader to admit a name no embedder can register, and the rename the caps then ask for leaves that entry matching nothing ([#464])
- **`infc` now refuses a host import at a target that binds no host — `stellar` — before external resolution, beside the proof refusal.** Asked only in code generation, it came after two refusals whose remedies it then made void: a mixed program was told to bind every extern to `host::…`, and a build under an allowlist that did not admit the import was told to admit it, leaving a policy entry nothing could use. The words are code generation's, through the target half of its own check, now public as `inference_wasm_codegen::check_host_import_target_support`, so there is no third wording to keep in step; code generation still asks the same question as the backstop for any other caller. Raised there, the refusal opens `Error: ` rather than the backstop's `Codegen failed: `, since code generation has not run ([#464])
- **A build that ships host imports prints one line naming every pair it will ask an embedder for**, sorted by module and then field, where a build that merged externals prints `Linked N external module(s)` — the two are mutually exclusive, since a program binding both kinds is refused at resolution. The line reads `host imports: env.clock_ms, fprime_core.telemetry` under an allowlist and `host imports (no allowlist): …` without one, and the parenthetical is not decoration: the two builds are otherwise byte-identical and print the same names, so it is the only thing that tells a reviewer reading a build log whether a reviewed policy admitted these imports or nothing did. It is also the only compile-time surface that reports the reservation's silent reinterpretation — a two-segment `use { f } from host::io;` clause that used to link `host/io.wasm` now declares a host import of `io`, reads no file, and still succeeds — so it names every pair rather than counting them. The pairs are rendered `env.clock_ms` rather than the backticked spelling a diagnostic uses, because a build-log line is read as plain text and its names are copied back into `--host-imports` — inside quotes, since the shell would otherwise split the list at the space after each comma ([#464])
- `infc`'s link-step failure now opens `Link step failed: ` where it opened `Linking external modules failed: `. The step no longer always merges external modules: a program whose externs are host imports has nothing to merge, and its artifact is checked and shipped by the same step, so a failure there is not a failure to link anything in. *Migration*: a test or script matching the old prefix has to be updated; the message after the prefix is unchanged for every pre-existing failure ([#464])
- `infc --target spacewasm` is accepted and builds for the SpaceWasm flight interpreter; compiler ABI minor bumped 6 → 7, which is the minor the `spacewasm` *name* became requestable at. This supersedes the `[#324]` `### CLI` bullet below recording the 5 → 6 bump, which is left as written per this file's append-and-leave practice; both bumps are name-only minors and neither adds a flag. What the flag does is narrow, not transform: the module written is byte-for-byte the one `--target wasm32` writes from the same source — nothing on the emission path reads a target — and what the name buys is the refusals. `--mode proof`, and a bare `-v` which implies it, are refused by code generation, because the interpreter's decoder does not define the custom `0xfc` instructions; `--wasm-features bulk-memory` is refused for the same kind of reason, the decoder having implemented no post-MVP proposal. `--mode compile -v` is **not** refused, unlike at `--target stellar`, and writes a `.v` byte-identical to the `wasm32` one: there is no post-translation rewrite here for the translation to run ahead of. That is now a pinned decision rather than a consequence of the Stellar refusal naming one target — widening that refusal to "any non-default target" would take it away, and the CLI suite goes red if it does. `infc --abi-version` prints `1.7`, and an unknown `--target` value now lists three accepted names ([#325])
- `infc --target stellar` is accepted and produces a deployable Soroban smart contract; compiler ABI minor bumped 5 → 6, which is the minor the `stellar` *name* became requestable at. What the flag now does past selecting a target: after linking, and before any file is written, the module is rewritten into the Stellar value ABI — one appended wrapper per exported method taking and returning the host's 64-bit tagged word, the export section retargeted onto the wrappers, every existing function keeping its index and its bytes, and a `contractenvmetav0` custom section declaring environment protocol 20 appended last. A build prints one summary line beside its progress lines naming each method with its value arity, the declared protocol and the byte size — `Stellar contract: add/2, flip/1, nothing/0; env protocol 20; 359 bytes` — because a Soroban host reports a wrong-arity call without naming the arity it expected and reports a wrong-typed argument as an undiscriminated trap, so the build log is where a caller finds out what to send. A build for the default target is unchanged down to the byte: the rewrite is a branch in the write path that the default never takes, and code generation itself never reads the target. Two earlier `[Unreleased]` bullets are superseded rather than edited, per this file's append-and-leave practice: the `[build] target` validation bullet under `### Breaking` quotes the load diagnostic's trailing sentence as naming `wasm32` alone, which now lists both names, and the `--target` bullet below records `stellar` and `soroban` as refused reserved names — `stellar` now resolves, and `soroban` is refused as its former spelling with a message redirecting to it ([#324])
- `infc -v` is refused together with `--target stellar`, naming the divergence rather than writing both files. `--mode proof` and a bare `-v` were already refused (the target cannot decode the custom 0xfc instructions proof mode emits); `--mode compile -v` was not, and it kept compile mode and ran the Rocq translation on the **linked, pre-rewrite** bytes while the `.wasm` written beside the `.v` was the post-rewrite contract — a proof artifact describing a module with different exports, different bodies and no metadata section, claiming to be about the one on disk. The refusal runs before any phase, so neither artifact appears, and it points at the build that does produce a usable pair: the same source at `--target wasm32`, whose bytes are exactly what the Stellar build was rewritten from ([#324])
- Add `infc --target <name>`, naming the runtime the emitted module is built for; compiler ABI minor bumped 4 → 5 to advertise the additive flag. The vocabulary holds one name today, `wasm32`, and `--target wasm32` is byte-identical to omitting the flag — nothing on the emission path reads it — so what the flag ships is the selection surface itself: the shared name vocabulary, the ABI minor that lets `infs` tell whether an `infc` reaches a given name, and the manifest validation above. A name is matched exactly and case-sensitively with no whitespace trimmed, and every rejection names the surface, lists the supported set, and names surrounding whitespace as the cause when that is what happened; the flag is an `Option<String>` resolved by the shared vocabulary rather than a clap `value_enum`, because clap's "possible values" list can carry neither that sentence nor the next one. `stellar` and `soroban` are refused as reserved: both spell a target the compiler carries in code generation, so the generic "unknown target" would be misleading, and they instead read `is a code-generation target with no user-facing name yet, and its output is not yet deployable`. Each name records the ABI minor it became requestable at rather than sharing one floor, so a caller asks whether an `infc` reaches *this name* — a single floor would wave a later name past a compiler that parses the flag and cannot build for it ([#324])
- Add `infc --adopt-external-specs`, carrying a linked library's **universal** (`forall`) proof obligations into the program's proof artifact so a library's own `ValidSpec` obligations about the bodies the merge splices in reach the emitted `.v`; compiler ABI minor bumped 3 → 4 to advertise the additive flag. Each adopted specification is namespaced under the logical module it was bound as — `mathlib` plus `DoubleSpec` gives `mathlib_DoubleSpec`, `a::b` gives `a_b_DoubleSpec` — and every function symbol its obligations apply is resolved in the library's own name section, required to be one of the bodies this merge actually folded in, and rewritten to the exact string the merged name section carries for that body, through the same `MergedFunc::name` field the encoder reads rather than a second `format!` that could drift from it. Adoption fails closed on every ambiguity: a name colliding with one the program declares or with another library's, a name the proof translation cannot spell as an identifier, a symbol the library's own name section carries on no function or on several, an obligation over a function this merge did not need, and a library whose own two verification sections disagree are each a hard `LinkError` naming the library, the specification and the symbol. `exists`/`unique` obligations are **not** adopted and each one left behind is reported: their judgments are evaluated against the frame an execution of the specification function reaches, and that function is precisely what the merge does not fold in. A library whose obligations are all reachability obligations contributes no specification at all rather than an empty one, since a `ValidSpec` over an empty list is true of every module and would state nothing about the program. Off by default, and refused outright outside proof mode rather than accepted as a no-op, because compile mode strips the program's own specification functions and emits no verification section for the obligations to join ([#362])
- Add `infc --out-dir <path>` flag to redirect compilation artifacts (default `out/` unchanged); compiler ABI minor version bumped 0 → 1 to advertise the additive flag ([#223])
- `infc -v` (and `infs build -v`) now implies `--mode proof` when no explicit `--mode` is passed; pass `--mode compile -v` explicitly for the prior stripped-specs behavior ([issue#22])
- Add `infc --mode proof` and `infs build --mode proof` to enable Rocq translation output; default remains `compile` mode with stripped specs ([issue#22])
- `infc` now surfaces `WasmToVError::RocqStdlibShadow` and `WasmToVError::InvalidRocqIdentifier` with dedicated user-facing messages ([issue#20])
- Running `infc` or `infs build` without phase flags now performs full compilation and writes `out/<name>.wasm`, matching conventional compiler UX ([#138])
- Add `BuildProfile` (Debug/Release) with `resolve_opt_level()` for target-aware optimization ([issue#97])
- Remove external toolchain dependencies: no `inf-llc`, `rust-lld`, or platform-specific library paths required ([#125])
- Defer WASM compilation until output files are actually needed (`-o` or `-v` flags) ([issue#97])
- Refactor CLI architecture with improved argument handling ([#28])

### Rocq Translation

- New proof-mode diagnostic **`P017`** refuses arithmetic a specification body cannot carry, in two wordings keyed on the quantifier of the *function* rather than on lexical containment in a `spec` block. A `forall` or plain body — a helper `fn` declared inside a `spec` included, since it is translated into an obligation of its own — becomes a term, and a term's `+`, `-` and `*` are `Wasm_int.int_add`/`int_sub`/`int_mul`, modular at the operand width: no second operator exists downstream for `checked(...)` or `wrapping(...)` to select, so both spellings would translate to the identical term and be dropped without a word, and both are therefore refused with a message saying so and pointing at the `assume` envelope that states the range instead. An `exists`/`unique` body is the opposite case: it is compiled and its obligation is a claim about running it, so an operator whose *effective* mode traps puts a trap on the path the judgment reduces, and because the judgment fixes an arbitrary typed entry vector before letting the choices range, that trap makes the theorem false rather than narrowing it — worse in a `unique` body, where a trapping choice shrinks the successful set and can make a uniqueness claim hold for a reason the source does not state. `wrapping(...)` is accepted throughout such a body, its final `assert` included, and translates to the term the unmarked spelling produces, which is what makes it a remedy rather than a second rejection. The check is a body scan run before translation rather than a case in the term walk, because an annotation in an array index or an aggregate-literal element is folded through on its way to a value and would otherwise be accepted at exactly the positions where it vanishes most quietly. A nested `exists { }` block inside a `forall` function is not a reachability body and takes the first wording. The second wording names the operator as the author spelled it: an operator left unmarked is reported as having "no place unmarked" in the body, and one written `checked(...)` as having no place there "inside a `checked(...)`", read from the innermost annotation governing it rather than from the language's default — a reader told their marked expression is unmarked is being told about a program they did not write. The clause beside it, saying what arithmetic does elsewhere in the language, is the default's and moves with it ([#316])
- New proof-mode diagnostic **`P018`** refuses an `exists`/`unique`-quantified specification function whose body reaches, through calls, a function carrying an overflow guard. `P017`'s second wording is lexical, and the trap it describes does not have to be written in the body to be taken by it: the judgment reduces the whole activation, callee frames included, so a guard one call deep empties the same observation set and makes the same claim false. Resolution is code generation's own identity path, refactored into scope-parameterized helpers both the emitter and this walk call, and re-scoped at every hop to the callee's own file and enclosing specification — a walk holding the specification's own scope fixed would resolve hop two's bare names in hop zero's file and pick the wrong function whenever two files define the same name. An instance method is an ordinary hop rather than a callee the rule declines to follow. A callee code generation cannot resolve is counted as carrying a guard, because failing open would ship a false obligation green through a gate that admits open proofs. `external fn` callees are skipped: code generation never receives a dependency's bytes, and the link-time check is the sole judge of a merged body. The rule is a reachability question and not a dataflow one, because whether the value that reaches a guard comes from an entry is not visible at the call, and a rule whose reach a reader cannot determine from the site it fires on is worse than a stricter one they can ([#316])
- A new standing audit over the emitted-obligation corpus, `no_universal_obligation_applies_an_unbounded_guarded_callee`, covers the one falsification shape the toolchain can recognise without a prover. `HA_app_ok` unfolds downstream to total correctness, so a universal obligation applying a callee that *traps* at some admitted argument vector is **false** rather than vacuous — `strictify` demands `HA_defined` of the applied term and `interp_realized_universal` is one-directional, so there is no arm in which the claim quietly narrows to the arguments that work. The audit therefore requires every universal obligation that applies a guarded callee to bound, in an antecedent, the variables it applies, or to appear on an exemption list whose entries must claim the obligation is *true* rather than that the violation is known. It reads the compiler's own records — the obligation map for the applications, the guarded-function list for the callees — rather than the emitted text, because a regular expression over a `.v` would measure the printer instead of the program, and it counts the applications it examined and fails on a count of zero, so it cannot pass by looking at nothing. Only frame slots are tracked as variables: a logical variable is a de Bruijn index rewritten to its own binder depth, so the same source variable is a different index in an antecedent than in the consequent, and an argument that is one is outside the audit's reach in both directions. This is a test rather than a diagnostic because the property is undecidable from the source ([#316])
- `core/wasm-to-v/ROCQ_CONTRACT.md`'s § *Trap-freedom* gains the overflow guard as a seventh `BI_unreachable` role — six emitting sites now, and the section says which of the roles an obligation can usefully range over. Four further statements land there, each previously true but unwritten. That add/sub/mul/neg emit **no trap site** under a wrapping effective mode, so an `HA_app_ok` over such a callee constrains nothing about overflow and its silence must not be read as coverage. The **trap-totality law** beside the entry-totality law: a reachability body must, at every typed entry vector, admit at least one choice vector on which nothing in the whole activation traps — stated for every trap kind, since `P016`, `P017` and `P018` each enforce only a part of it. The **universal-obligation rule** the corpus audit exists for. And the two *native* arithmetic traps the role table does not and cannot cover — a zero divisor at every width, and signed `MIN / -1` at `i32` and `i64`, which reports `wasm trap: integer overflow`, a string that appeared in no in-tree markdown file before ([#316])
- The `P0xx` registry range is now `P001`–`P018` in `core/wasm-to-v/ROCQ_CONTRACT.md`, `core/wasm-codegen/README.md` and `core/wasm-codegen/docs/specification-obligations.md`, whose own table was missing `P001`, `P006`, `P009`, `P010`, `P011`, `P012` and `P013` and now carries every code the contract does ([#316])
- An adopted external specification appears in the emitted `.v` as `Definition <module>__<logical module>_<spec>_specs : list hassert` plus `Theorem valid_<module>__<logical module>_<spec> : ValidSpec <module> …`, with each `T_app` naming the merged body at its post-link `mod_funcs` ordinal. Its `inference.spec_funcs` entry carries an **empty** index list, because the library's own specification function never crossed the merge — the shape was verified end to end against `coqc`, not assumed: the empty list contributes nothing to `omitted`, so the applied merged body stays in the module record, while `sorted_spec_names` still emits the full per-spec grammar from the obligations alone. The translator is unchanged; `ROCQ_CONTRACT.md` now records that an empty index list has two producers, a method-free `spec S { }` from code generation and an adopted specification from the linker. Adoption carries obligations and never discharges them: every adopted theorem arrives as an `Admitted.` skeleton to be proved against the merged module, which is what makes it sound across the shared linear memory, remapped globals and renumbered calls the merge introduces ([#362])
- Generated proof skeletons now end in `Admitted.` directly, so the
  `coqc` gate type-checks its raw generated input without rewriting
  `Qed.` terminators. The admission is deliberately a proof hole:
  downstream completion replaces it with proof tactics ending in `Qed.`,
  and an admission/assumption audit remains required before claiming
  proof closure. No theorem statements, obligations, WASM output,
  compile mode, or CLI behavior changed. Migration: consumers that
  matched or rewrote generated `Qed.` must accept `Admitted.` and add the
  closure audit ([#358])

- A data segment's bytes are now spelled `(encode N%Zst)` for every value, and a module carrying one no longer emits `Open Scope byte_scope.`. The previous emission used coq-wasm's two-digit hex notations where they exist, which **did not type-check against the real library** for any byte containing a hex digit `A`-`F`: upstream declares `Notation "#FF" := (encode (#F * 16 + #F))` over `Notation "#F" := 15`, a `nat` literal, while `encode : Z -> byte` — so an emitted module opening `byte_scope` but not `Z_scope` was rejected with `The term "15" has type "nat" while it is expected to have type "Z"`. Only bytes whose two hex digits are both `0`-`9`, minus the twelve values upstream's block skips, survived: **92 of 256**. The vendored signature stub hid this completely, because its own hand-written notation block is declared over its own opaque `encode`, so every gate was green while the artifact the prover consumes was unparseable. Measured after the change: all 32 modules the gate builds type-check against the real coq-wasm v2.2.0 + wasm-verifier libraries, including the two carrying data segments. No committed golden moves — no `.inf` corpus fixture produces a data segment, so the shape was reachable only through a hand-assembled module ([#359], refs [#346])

- A spec carrying an `exists` obligation no longer aliases a sibling spec's universal list. The gathering list was emitted as `<mod>__<Spec>_ex_specs : list reachability_spec`, which is spelled identically to the universal `<mod>__<Spec>_ex_specs : list hassert` of a sibling spec literally named `<Spec>_ex` — **one name, two `Definition`s, two different types, in one file**, written at exit 0 with no diagnostic and refused only by `coqc` with `Error: <name> already exists.`; `unique` aliased a `<Spec>_uq` sibling the same way. The two list suffixes now join with the reserved `__` run, so the aliasing is impossible by construction rather than rejected after the fact: a spec name may neither contain `__` nor end in `_`, so `<Spec>__ex_specs` is a string no sibling can forge. An exhaustive tail-suffix analysis over the whole emitted-name surface found exactly two collidable pairs — `_specs` against `_ex_specs` and against `_uq_specs` — and both witnesses become inadmissible under the doubled join, so only those two suffixes move; the per-obligation `_exspec{k}`/`_uqspec{k}` records keep their single `_` because they end in a decimal digit where a list ends in `s`, and all three theorem names are provably collision-free. The name is spelled in two independent places, the definition site (which builds it from a suffix binding and so is invisible to a grep for `_ex_specs`) and the theorem body, so a partial edit would emit a theorem naming a list that does not exist. `rocq_name_collisions.inf` grows both colliding pairs; no corpus entry could reach the shape before, because it needs one spec whose name is another spec's name followed by the emitter's own tag. Migration: a downstream proof naming a reachability list renames it to the doubled form ([#364])
- A proof-mode `.v` now carries a real bounds obligation for a dynamic array access, and it is one the contract already had rather than a new predicate. The retained trap gives the emitted module a site to be about, and `HA_app_ok f τs` — the atom a **bare call statement** in a specification body emits, at any result arity including void — is what forces it: downstream it denotes `val_fun V f vs <> None`, `interp_realized` turns that into `fun_computes m f vs rs`, and `fun_computes` is defined through `sem_mx`, a total-correctness judgment whose conclusion demands the body reduce to a *value stack*, never to `AI_trap`. A realization claim is therefore a trap-freedom claim per (function, argument vector), scoped exactly where a programmer asked for it by writing an envelope and claiming a call is total on it. Giving `ValidModule` a "no reachable trap" component instead is rejected as false by construction: `ValidModule` is WasmCert's structural `module_typing` judgment and reduces nothing, and `BI_unreachable` is emitted from five sites in six roles — a value-returning function's dead tail, an ordinary executable `assert`, a reachability filter in a retained body, the bounds guard, the narrow signed-division overflow guard, and an exported entry's `enum` tag guard — so a blanket clause would make `assert` unusable in proof mode and falsify every reachability obligation at once. New fatal proof-mode diagnostic **P016**: a non-constant array index inside an `exists`/`unique` body, whose obligation the guard makes **false** rather than narrower — the reachability predicates fix an arbitrary typed entry vector before the choices range, so an entry whose index trips the guard leaves an empty observation set, the same failure an `assume` over an entry parameter produces; suppressing the guard there instead was rejected, because the body the judgment reduces would stop being the body that ships. New corpus fixture `spec_bounds_realization.inf` and its golden carry the pair end to end through the `coqc` gate, with needles on both halves — an assertion on the obligation alone passes just as well against an unguarded artifact. The full statement of which obligation carries trap-freedom, and why neither module-level judgment can, is a new section in `core/wasm-to-v/ROCQ_CONTRACT.md`. Unaffected: a *literal* index is guarded in no mode, and its static story stays **A037** plus **P014**. The boundary is narrower than "constant" suggests and worth stating: code generation folds a direct number literal and nothing else, so `a[K]` for a `const K` and `a[1 + 1]` are guarded in both modes, while the translator's own fold treats them as constant. Two consequences fall out. In an executable function `const K: i32 = 5; a[K]` over an `[i32; 3]` has no static story at all — A037 wants the literal directly under the access, P014 runs only on specification bodies — so the guard is now the only thing that catches it, a hole this change closes. And inside an `exists`/`unique` body, where a live trap would be fatal, `a[K]` passes **P016** and is guarded anyway; that guard is dead, because **P014** has already checked the folded index against the array's declared length, which is the length the guard compares against. The symbolic range bound a *specification-body* index carries is a definedness rule about a term and is untouched, a non-constant index in a `forall`/plain body still translates, and every previously committed `.v` golden is byte-identical. Migration: move a non-constant index out of an `exists`/`unique` body, or make it constant ([#214])
- A function whose name collides with something the emitted `.v` already defines is now disambiguated instead of claiming the name twice. Every generated file opens with eight fixed preamble helpers (`Vi32`, `Vi64`, `Mt`, `Mm`, `Mg`, `Mi`, `Me`, `Ma`), then one `Definition` per function, then `Definition <module> : module` and `Theorem valid_<module>`. Nothing checked these against each other, so **any `.v` whose module name equalled one of its function names defined that name twice and Rocq refused the whole file** — `coqc` reporting `Error: <name> already exists.` while `infc` exited 0 and printed "V generated at". The module name is the entry file's stem, so this fired on `calc.inf` containing `fn calc`, and on `main.inf` containing `pub fn main()` — the canonical program shape, which therefore has never produced a compilable proof artifact. Function names now seed the existing index-suffixing disambiguator (the one a static merge already relies on when it folds an external library's private function beside a same-named local one), so `main.inf` emits `main_0` for the function and keeps `main` for the module record. A *module* name colliding with a preamble helper (`Me.inf`) is rejected instead, with a rename hint: it is the artifact's identity, the `ValidModule` subject, and the prefix of every spec-derived proof name, so there is nothing to rename it to. The reserved set is complete rather than sampled — the preamble emits exactly those eight, and every other emitted top-level name is the module record, `valid_<module>`, or carries the `__` separator that name sanitization collapses. The `coqc` gate could not have caught this: every `CORPUS` row pairs a stem-derived module name with fixtures whose entry function is `main`, so the two never coincided; a new `rocq_name_collisions.inf` fixture contesting all three shapes closes that blindness. This is the only user-facing change in [#365] — every other defect below is reachable only through the public `translate_bytes` API, because the linker re-encodes the type section on every CLI path ([#365])
- `wasm-to-v` now fails closed on every foreign or malformed `.wasm` instead of emitting an ill-formed `.v` or aborting. **The type section was the headline defect**: a GC `struct`/`array`/`cont` composite hit an empty `//TODO` arm returning `Ok("")`, which the caller spliced into `mod_types` with its list separator, emitting a dangling `::` — a Gallina parse error, at exit 0. Two further sources of the same shape were found alongside it and are closed by the same restructure: an empty `(rec)` contributed a phantom element, and a recursion group defining several types was concatenated into **one** list element, so `Tf a b Tf c d` was over-application *and* every later type index shifted against what `defined_func_param_count` already assumed. `translate_function_type` now yields one element per flattened entry, making a `mod_types` position the WASM type index by construction; declared subtyping (`sub`, non-final) and shared composites are refused rather than silently discarded. **Five aborts are gone**: unwraps on the operator stream, an `if` arm truncated by end-of-stream, a truncated locals vector, and a truncated function section each killed the process, and a seven-byte locals declaration advertising `0xFFFFFFFF` repetitions drove an unbounded `String` until the OS killed the process — a signal no `?` can recover from, now capped at the parser fork's own `MAX_WASM_FUNCTION_LOCALS` (re-exported rather than copied, so the bound cannot drift), accumulated *across* locals groups so several legal-looking groups cannot sum past it. A sixth site swallowed a failed locals reader and emitted `modfunc_locals := nil` for a function that has locals. **Two injection vectors are closed**: an import or export name carrying `"` closed its Gallina string literal early and the remainder was read as code — an export named `a" (MED_func 99%N) :: Me "b` emitted *two* exports from one — and a name-section local name carrying `*)` closed its Coq comment early, injecting a `BI_unreachable` the binary did not contain.
