Names are escaped (quotes doubled) and comment delimiters neutralized surgically, never routed through identifier sanitization, which would rewrite `__frame_ptr` and move every array-using program's golden. **Shapes the record cannot carry are refused** rather than emitted as their nearest neighbour: `table64` and shared tables (the table-side of a guard the memory helper twelve lines below already had), tables with an element initializer, and any memory index other than `0` — guarded once in `translate_memarg`, which every load and store routes through, plus the three bulk-memory operators that name their memories directly. **Structural contradictions are now checked**: a component binary shares the core preamble's first four bytes and used to arrive as an empty core module complete with its validity theorem; repeated and out-of-order core sections (ordered by an explicit rank table, since the ids are *not* monotonic in the required order — data count carries id 12 but belongs between element and code); a data count disagreeing with its data section; function and code sections of different lengths, which had their missing entries defaulted to type 0; and operators after a body's terminating `end`, which were silently dropped. No `Validator` is constructed at the boundary, deliberately — the crate's own rejection fixtures are stack-invalid on purpose and must still translate — so every check is per-site. Every rejection is a typed `WasmToVError` behind a prefix this crate owns, guarded by a totality test that fails if any one wrap is removed. Unaffected: every committed `.v` golden is byte-identical, and Inference codegen emits none of these shapes ([#365]) instead of refusing them, and the vendored `coqc` stub grows the three declarations they need. The five sign-extension operators emit `BI_unop t (Unop_extend n)` — the proof contract classifies sign-extension as a **unop**, beside `clz`/`ctz`/`popcnt`, not as a conversion, so the WASM mnemonics group them misleadingly and that misgrouping is why they were retracted alongside the conversion block under [#284]. The three integer-to-integer conversions emit `BI_cvtop`: `BI_cvtop T_i32 CVO_wrap T_i64 None`, `BI_cvtop T_i64 CVO_extend T_i32 (Some SX_S)`, and the `SX_U` variant — the only three instances the backend's `cvtop_valid` admits. The stub gains `Unop_extend : N -> unop`, a `cvtop` inductive, and `BI_cvtop` appended to `basic_instruction`; `cvtop` declares only `CVO_wrap` and `CVO_extend`, because the backend's six remaining constructors each need a float number type the stub deliberately does not declare, and a stub narrower than the backend keeps an accidental emission an unbound-constructor error rather than a silently type-checking term. `Unop_extend`'s argument is the source width in **bits**, not bytes: the backend's `unop_type_agree` ignores the argument entirely while its `app_unop` divides by eight, so a byte count elaborates, satisfies the typing side condition, and denotes the constant zero for every input — a provable-but-false obligation no `coqc` gate can see, pinned instead by byte comparison against `Unop_extend 8%N`/`16%N`/`32%N` in `tests/src/rocq_typecheck.rs` and `core/wasm-to-v/src/lib.rs`. Still refused: every float instruction, every conversion naming a float (`trunc`, `trunc_sat`, `convert`, `demote`, `promote`, `reinterpret`), and the float/vector value types; the rejection message now says why (no float number type) rather than claiming the contract covers no conversion at all. Unaffected: Inference codegen emits none of the eight, so every committed `.v` golden and every corpus output is byte-identical, and the new terms reach the `.v` only from foreign or statically-linked `.wasm` — a hand-assembled module added to the `coqc` gate is what elaborates them ([#363])
- Specifications can now state aggregate, element-wise and alternating-quantifier properties directly, instead of being rejected outright. A compound `@`, a compound parameter, an array or struct literal, and a copy of one are translated to a shape-preserving tree of *scalar leaves*: one universal slot with its own `HA_has_type` guard per leaf (one `HA_ex` binder per leaf in an existential context), enumerated arrays row-major and struct fields in layout order, allocated parameters-first-in-declaration-order then each `@` in binding order. So `let a: [i32; 3] = @; assert(a[0] <= a[0]);` produces a real obligation where it was **P008**, `a[0]` and `p.x` resolve against that tree at translation time where they were **P002**, and aggregate `==`/`!=` in assertion position is the leafwise conjunction (or its De Morgan dual) — `==` compares values, and an aggregate's value is exactly its ordered leaves. The supported surface is deliberately equal to the executable aggregate `@` surface, since proof mode lowers spec bodies through the same unrolling: arrays of scalars at any rank, and structs whose fields are scalars or one-dimensional scalar arrays. Arrays of structs (**A028**) and structs with struct or multidimensional-array fields (**A027**) stay rejected on every specification path — `@`, parameters and literals alike — so neither surface is wider than the other. An index the translation cannot fold binds a witness pinned by the unsigned range bound `i <u N` **first**, then one implication per element, which is what makes bounded iteration expressible: a `forall` binding an array and an index, assuming a bound on every element and a range for the index, and asserting the bound at that index emits a real obligation that discharges against the verifier (`tests/test_data/inf/spec_bounded_iteration.inf`). Out of range that definition is unsatisfiable and the enclosing atom is refuted — `a[i]` denotes *the element at index `i`, which exists*, a definedness rule about a term the obligation constructs rather than a mirror of the runtime guard a compiled access carries — a specification body is never compiled, and [#214]'s guard would not change this bound; the alternative, a guarded implication leaving an out-of-range read vacuously satisfied, is exactly what **P010** rejects elsewhere. Constant steps of a chain descend first, so `m[1][j]` splits over the selected row, while two non-constant steps in one chain are **P002**. A `forall` block inside an `exists`/`assume` block of a `forall`/plain spec function now emits a real universal binder — new IR node `HAssert::All`, printed as wasm-verifier's derived `Hall`, carried on new `inference.hspecs` assert tag `0x0B` (section version stays **2**: the change is additive, an older decoder fails loudly with `UnknownHassertTag(0x0B)`, the linker decodes hspecs only from the main module, and recompilation rather than migration is the compatibility story) and declared in the vendored `coqc` stub. A free slot there would have been quantified by `ValidSpec`'s outer universal, turning `∃k. ∀x. P` into `∀x. ∃k. P` silently — which is why **P007** is lifted only for that nesting and kept inside `exists`/`unique`-quantified bodies, where every `@` is a hidden choice parameter the judgment quantifies operationally. Two new fatal diagnostics come with the encoding: **P013**, a per-spec-function running budget of 64 quantified scalar leaves (each leaf costs a binder level and a guard level, and the levels accumulate across every introduction in the function, so a per-introduction cap would not bound the shared assertion-depth budget; checked from the declared type before any leaf is materialized), and **P014**, a constant-*folded* out-of-bounds index (`const K: i32 = 5; a[K]`, `a[1 + 4]`), stating the same fact **A037** states for a direct-literal index at the spellings A037's pattern cannot see. One usability trap is inherent to the definedness rule and is documented rather than diagnosed: the emitted range bound is unsigned, so a *signed* index needs both `0 <= i` and `i < N` before its element denotes at all — supplying only the upper bound compiles clean and yields an obligation that is false, surfacing later as an unprovable Rocq goal. The two bounds are necessary, not sufficient: they make the element exist, while a claim about its *value* still needs hypotheses about the element's value, since a compound `@` states only the typing of its leaves. A `u32` index needs no lower bound and yields a simpler obligation. Unaffected: every committed `.v` golden is byte-identical, as is every proof-mode `.wasm` (the pass is read-only over the typed AST), and compile mode has no obligations; no previously emitted obligation shape changes, so no downstream proof needs restating. Migration: none — programs that compiled still compile, and programs that were rejected may now translate ([#355])
- A data byte with no `byte_scope` notation now reaches the emitted `.v` as `(encode 18%Zst)` instead of `(encode 18%Z)`, and a module carrying a data segment claims that key in its preamble with a second conditional line — `Local Delimit Scope Z_scope with Zst.`, next to the `Open Scope byte_scope.` it already emitted. `%Zst` is a private delimiting key for the same standard `Z_scope` the literal always lived in (a Rocq scope may carry several keys), so the term denotes exactly what it denoted before. It is claimed because the `Z` key is not reliably `Z_scope`'s: mathcomp's algebra library delimits its own `int_scope` with it (`ssrint.v`, alongside a `Number Notation` on `int`), so in any file whose `Import`/`Export` chain applies that `Delimit` the argument of `(encode 18%Z)` re-reads as mathcomp's `int` and stops elaborating against `encode : Z -> byte`. Two files can be that file — the emitted `.v` itself, should the backend build behind its preamble ever re-export mathcomp algebra, and a consumer that imports it and restates emitted data bytes in its own text. Delimiting is last-writer-wins — whichever `Delimit` the chain applies last decides the key; an explicit `%Z` is read through the key regardless of which scopes are open, so `Open Scope Z_scope.` recovers nothing, and nothing a consumer writes downstream repairs a file that failed to compile on its own. Re-delimiting is the one measure that works, and taking a private key rather than taking `Z` back leaves mathcomp's own `%Z` intact for anything read alongside the module, while `Local` keeps the key from leaking to consumers that import the file. Unaffected: the 244 byte values that have a notation keep their `#78` spelling, and every module without a data segment is byte-identical, preamble included — Inference codegen emits no data segment, so no committed `.v` golden or corpus output moves and only foreign or statically-linked `.wasm` reaches the changed spelling. A downstream file that restates emitted data bytes in its own text must carry the same `Local Delimit` line ([#416])
- The emitted `Ma` memarg helper in every Rocq `.v` file now binds `ofs al` instead of `of al` (`Definition Ma ofs al := {|memarg_offset := ofs; memarg_align := al|}.`): `of` is an ordinary identifier in vanilla Rocq but a keyword under ssreflect, so a consumer importing mathcomp ahead of the emitted definitions hit a parse error on that preamble line before reaching anything the file states. `Ma` is always applied positionally (`Ma 0%N 2%N`), so no call site or downstream proof changes, and every emitted `.v` differs by exactly this one line ([#412])
- `wasm-to-v` no longer drops a table import's element type. `translate_module_import_desc`'s `TypeRef::Table` arm applied the contract's `MID_table : table_type -> module_import_desc` to a bare `limits` record instead of the two-field `table_type` (`{|tt_limits; tt_elem_type|}`) the constructor actually takes — the shape looked right because its neighbour `MID_mem` really does take a bare `limits`, since the contract's `memory_type` *is* `limits`. `coqc` rejects the result outright (`The term "{| lim_min := …; lim_max := … |}" has type "limits" while it is expected to have type "table_type"`), so any table import produced a `.v` that does not type-check. No fixture in the `coqc` gate had ever imported a table — Inference codegen does emit an import for every `extern fn`, but the static-merge linker (`inference-wasm-linker`) is fail-closed on imports (`LinkError::UnsatisfiedImport`), and `infc` runs that link, aborting on failure, before `-v` translation starts, so no import from the normal pipeline ever reaches the translator — so the arm was reachable only from a foreign or hand-assembled `.wasm` fed to `wasm_to_v` directly, and the defect went uncaught until the [#401] coverage work's `module_surface` fixture became the first gated module to import one. `MID_table` is now applied to `{|tt_limits := <limits>; tt_elem_type := <reference_type>|}`, pinned by both a `funcref` and an explicit-max `externref` table import in a new unit test (`an_imported_table_carries_its_element_type`, `core/wasm-to-v/src/lib.rs`) and by the `module_surface` gate fixture. Unaffected: every other import descriptor, and every committed corpus `.v` golden, since none of Inference codegen's own imports reach `wasm_to_v` — the fail-closed linker resolves them, or the build aborts, before translation runs ([#402])
- Element segments, data segments, and `br_table` now emit terms the proof backend accepts, and the vendored `coqc` stub now mirrors the part of that backend's interface a data segment needs. The backend is wasm-verifier, whose WASM datatypes come from its `coq-wasm` (>= 2.2.0) dependency; three emitted terms had no constructor there. An element segment's function indexes were written as an `ME_functions` element *mode* into `modelem_init`, the field that holds initializer expressions — an outright `coqc` error, so any module carrying a function-index element segment produced an unusable `.v`. The other two type-checked only because the vendored stub had drifted along with the emitter — the false green its own README warns about — declaring `ME_declared` where the dependency has `ME_declarative`, and a one-argument `BI_br_table` that silently dropped the default label, making the emitted instruction a different instruction from the module's; that one was a hard error only for a table with no explicit targets, which emitted a bare, partially-applied `BI_br_table`. Now `BI_br_table` carries the label vector *and* the default (`BI_br_table (0%N :: 1%N :: nil) 1%N`, and `BI_br_table nil 0%N` for `br_table 0`), function-index element items desugar to the `BI_ref_func` initializer expressions `modelem_init` is typed for, `ME_declarative` takes the drifted spelling's place, and a `ref.func` operand is renumbered past omitted spec functions like every other function index, so both element item forms reach the same index instead of only the shorthand one. A data byte keeps its `#78` spelling, which is the dependency's own `byte_scope` notation; the defect there was on the stub side, which declared no such notation and so rejected in-repo what the backend accepts. The stub now mirrors `byte_scope`, the exported `encode : Z -> byte` its notations abbreviate, and the 244 of 256 two-digit uppercase notations the dependency's hand-written block actually declares — it skips `#12` .. `#19` and `#1C` .. `#1F`, so those twelve now reach the `.v` as the `encode` application the notation would have abbreviated (`(encode 18%Z)`) rather than as syntax that parses nowhere. Mirroring all 256 instead would have made the gate accept a module the backend rejects, the same false green in the other direction. The stub deliberately omits the module-level `Open Scope byte_scope.` the dependency leaves open for importers, so a module carrying at least one data segment emits that line itself rather than depending on an `Import` chain to supply it, and the gate proves it does. Modules with no data segment emit no such line and are byte-identical. All of these constructs are unreachable from Inference codegen and arrive only from foreign or statically-linked `.wasm`, so no committed `.v` golden or corpus output moves; a handcrafted foreign module added to the `coqc` gate covers them ([#346])
- The emitted `.v` now spells every index immediate with an explicit `%N` scope. Six sites in the `wasm-to-v` translation wrote structurally identical operands bare while their siblings wrote them scoped: `BI_br`, the `BI_br_table` label list elements, both operands of `BI_call_indirect`, `BI_memory_init`, `BI_data_drop`, and the `ME_functions` element-segment function index list (`BI_br 0` / `BI_br_table (0 :: 1 :: nil)` / `BI_call_indirect 0 0` / `BI_memory_init 0` / `BI_data_drop 0` / `ME_functions 0::nil` become `BI_br 0%N` / `BI_br_table (0%N :: 1%N :: nil)` / `BI_call_indirect 0%N 0%N` / `BI_memory_init 0%N` / `BI_data_drop 0%N` / `ME_functions 0%N::nil`). The proof contract types all of these as `N`, and Rocq's numeral notation is type-directed, so the bare spellings elaborated correctly and `coqc` accepted them — this fixes no bug today. It removes a latent one: the bare sites were correct only by scope inference, so a future contract or notation change that made the `N` scope non-inferable would break exactly and only them, and at the prover rather than in-repo. Spelling-only; no semantic change. Emitted bytes change for any module containing `br`, `br_table`, `call_indirect`, `memory.init`, `data.drop`, or a function-index element segment — of the committed `.v` goldens only `tests/test_data/rocq/rocq_prime_example.v` is affected (one line, from its `loop`), regenerated in this change. Everything but `br` is unreachable from Inference codegen, which emits none of those constructs; they reach the translator only from foreign or statically-linked `.wasm`, so no Inference program's output changes beyond the `BI_br` spelling ([#344])
- Negative integer constants now reach the emitted `.v` parenthesized — `BI_const_num (Vi32 (-1))`, not `BI_const_num (Vi32 -1)`. Gallina's `-` is an infix operator, so the old spelling parsed as the subtraction `Vi32 - 1` and `coqc` rejected the whole module ("The term `Vi32` has type `Z -> value_num` while it is expected to have type `nat`"), making proof mode unusable for any program containing a negative constant anywhere — an offset, a threshold, an error code. Every signed width is affected, since `i8`/`i16`/`i32` all lower to `i32.const` and `i64` to `i64.const`; so is source that writes no minus sign at all, because a `u32`/`u64` literal above the signed maximum is stored as a negative constant of that width. The `hassert` obligation printer already parenthesized its constants, so obligation terms are unchanged, as is every non-negative constant — no existing `.v` golden moves ([#314])
- WASM module-name subsection now reflects the CLI-supplied input file stem instead of the hardcoded `"output"`. The Rocq translator reads this back, so the emitted `Definition <mod>__<Spec>_specs` and `Theorem valid_<mod>` identifiers now use the source filename. Multi-module workflows that previously collided on a single `output` identifier now produce distinct ones
- Empty per-spec lists emit `(@nil hassert)` — not `[]%N`, and no longer `list N` at all — so the generated `Definition` type-checks regardless of whether a scope is active at the consumer's `Require` site. Downstream proof scripts matching `[]%N` or `(@nil N)` literally must update ([issue#21], [issue#22])
- Rewrite WASM-to-V translator for WasmCertCoq theory syntax ([#23])
- Add function name propagation to V output ([#24])

### Documentation

- `book/src/compilation_targets.md`'s Stellar section retitles "The metadata section and the declared protocol" as **The custom sections**, covering all three: `contractenvmetav0` (mandatory, refused at upload without it, 32 fixed bytes, protocol 20, read by the host), `contractspecv0` (every method's name, each parameter's name and type, and its return, read by tooling — `stellar contract invoke`, `stellar contract info interface` and `stellar contract bindings rust` — never by the host), and `contractmetav0` (the one `infver` entry, read by `stellar contract info meta` and by `soroban-spec`'s spec shaking for its own key). It states the append order — spec, then meta, then environment metadata last, so a contract still ends with the same 32 measured bytes — and, attributed to the CLI's own source, that `Spec::new` (`soroban-spec-tools` 28.0.0) decodes all three sections strictly and fails the whole command if any one does not, even though the host parses neither tooling section (measured in `tests/tests/stellar/envelope.rs`). The admissibility table gains a **Parameter name** row (any name but a lone `_`, at most 30 bytes, and no name that another parameter's kebab-case CLI flag alias spells; a leading underscore kept: `_x` is the flag `--_x`, and the CLI accepts its alias `--x` too, as the new "A leading underscore is kept" part of `MEASURED_ABI.md`'s "CLI measurement" chapter measures, which is why `x` beside `_x` is refused at build time), the refusal list quotes the three new messages from `core/wasm-codegen/src/lib.rs`, for a parameter written `_`, one named over 30 bytes, and a name that is another parameter's CLI flag alias, and a paragraph states that rule as the CLI's own, with the pairs measured failing and the pairs measured reliable. A new **Invoking a contract from the CLI** section reproduces from `MEASURED_ABI.md`'s "CLI measurement" chapter (commands and outputs quoted, elisions marked): `stellar contract info interface` rendering the interface in the SDK's own trait form, `stellar keys generate` funding a local key, `stellar contract deploy` returning a contract id, a named-argument `invoke` against it answering `42`, and one CLI-side type refusal — a mistyped argument rejected before any host call, naming the parameter and its declared type. The "What is not supported, and why" list drops its "No `contractspecv0` section" bullet, now false. In this file, the SpaceWasm section's contrast with Stellar now says the artifact carries "none of the contract sections a Stellar build appends" rather than "no metadata section", and the Stellar section's `[build.wasm-opt]` refusal in "What is not supported, and why" names `contractenvmetav0` explicitly rather than "the metadata section". `book/src/projects-and-the-infs-toolchain.md`'s two `[build.wasm-opt]` sentences — the Stellar-subsection load error and the SpaceWasm-subsection exemption — both now name a contract's wrappers and its `contractenvmetav0` section, matching the wording of the manifest's own load-time diagnostic; `apps/infs/docs/inference-toml.md` mirrors the same wording in its `[build] target` field reference. `core/stellar-abi/README.md` and the crate's `lib.rs` doc gain the same `Spec::new` sentence for `contractmetav0`, attributed to the CLI's own source ([#466])
- `book/src/the-wasm-linker.md` gives its import-free output the one exception the toolchain now ships. *Motivation* says a declared host import is the one import that survives, and that it survives by skipping the link — `inference::link_resolved` ships the code-generation bytes verbatim after proving the artifact's imports equal the declared host set — never by passing through the merge, which still produces a module with no import section; a program binding host imports and linked modules together is refused at external resolution, and a merge that lets a host import survive is not built. A new section, **The Host-Only Pass-Through**, says what that proof compares (module, field, parameters and results, in both directions, with a non-function import and a duplicated name each refused, and undecodable bytes refused rather than read as an empty import list), what the pass-through does not check because no merge runs (the main-module shapes the linker's gate rejects, the operator allow-list, tier classification and write-set derivation), and that the consistency check among the declarations at resolution stands in for `validate_extern`, pointing at the linking chapter rather than restating it ([#464])
- The SpaceWasm chapter of `book/src/compilation_targets.md` gains a **Host imports** section: the target admits the binding; `infc` asks the three registration caps of the embedder's registration API — 31-byte names, nine parameters, one result — of the declarations, after external resolution and before the allowlist, shown by a real transcript of the refusal a 32-byte module name earns, and the post-link conformance check asks them again of the bytes; such a program has no proof path; and the section points at the harness's `--host` transcript. The harness section names its two count refusals — more than 256 host module names, or more than 65,536 stubs under one — which the interpreter would otherwise bind to other stubs without a word. The Stellar host-import bullet says `infc` refuses the binding before external resolution and code generation refuses it again as the backstop, and gives the refusal's substance, where it said the refusal happened at code generation; its metadata paragraph ties "a contract imports nothing" to this target's refusal; and the proof-mode property table gains a **Host imports** row ([#464])
- `book/src/external-functions-and-wasm-linking.md`'s **Current Restrictions** lists what a program binding host imports cannot do: bind linked modules too, produce a proof artifact, have `mut` on a host parameter checked against what the embedder's function writes, build at `stellar`, bind an import name that is not an Inference identifier (`wasi_snapshot_preview1` can be bound; Soroban's `_` and `0` cannot be spelled), carry a contract, or run under `infs run` while the finished artifact, after any `[build.wasm-opt]` step, still imports a function. An entry that a **Host Imports** subsection or its refusal table already explains links there instead of restating it. The `infs run` transcript gains the `Error: ` prefix a real run prints, the section on running such a program points at the SpaceWasm harness, and **Related Resources** links the targets chapter's host-import section and the harness ([#464])
- `book/src/projects-and-the-infs-toolchain.md` says the unknown-key refusal is younger than every released `infs`, so a release through `v0.0.5` drops a `[host-imports]` table without a diagnostic and a project relying on its allowlist has to be built with an `infs` that knows the table ([#464])
- `apps/infs/docs/inference-toml.md` gains a `[host-imports]` section — the fields, the three policies, eager validation and what it refuses, the forward and its echo, the ABI gate, and the `infs run` refusal — and its unknown-keys, single-file and field-evolution sections record the table, including that every released `infs` through `v0.0.5` predates the unknown-key refusal and so drops the table silently. `book/src/external-functions-and-wasm-linking.md` describes the shipped table where it said the table was not yet written, adds a section on running a program that binds host imports — the `infs run` refusal, and the wasmtime `Linker::func_wrap` registration an embedder performs instead — and its refusal table names `infs` manifest validation beside `infc` argument handling for a `host` dependency key, and adds rows for a malformed table and for `infs run`. `book/src/projects-and-the-infs-toolchain.md` adds the table to its sample manifest and field table, a Reserved Module Segment paragraph beside the reserved project names, the `infs run` refusal, the minor-8 gate in place of the sentence saying nothing was gated on it, and — to its three spawn diagrams — the `--target`, memory, `--adopt-external-specs` and `--host-imports` lines they were missing. `apps/infs/README.md` gains the table row and the refusal. The manifest reference's unknown-keys section names the `host` segment a `[wasm-dependencies]` key may not open with, and its single-file table and the README's single-file paragraph list all five settings single-file mode honors, adding the `[build] target` and `[memory]` both had omitted ([#464])
- `book/src/external-functions-and-wasm-linking.md` gains a **Host Imports** section, and four paragraphs this feature had falsified are repaired in the same pass. The new section covers the surface and the one sentence that disambiguates `::` for a reader meeting it (`host::env` imports from the WASM module `env`; the `host` segment names the provider, not a path component); what the reservation does *not* touch — a `src/host/` directory, the file import `use host::x;`, and any variable, field or type named `host` — contrasted with the path grammar's own reserved `root` handle, including where the symmetry stops, since `root` shadows a literal `src/root.inf` while a `from host::…` clause consults no file at all; why exactly one segment may follow; the breaking change, including the two-segment clause that is now silently reinterpreted and the rename that is its remedy; that `mut` on a host parameter is the author's **assertion** rather than a contract the linker checks, with A047 still holding the call site to the same rooting rule and the write itself outside the verified artifact; that a bound host import is emitted whether or not the program calls it, and that an optimized build ships only the ones it calls, by design — `infs`'s opt-in `[build.wasm-opt]` step runs `wasm-opt -O<level>`, whose unused-element removal at every level but `0` drops an imported function nothing calls, and the build log then names each import removed and the set that ships; a table of what each layer refuses, and the order the refusals are raised in; the inventory line; the `[host-imports]` table `infs build` fills the flag from; and that no contract travels with a host import today, which is why a proof build is refused rather than qualified. The repairs: the chapter opened by promising a self-contained `.wasm`, which is true only of clauses naming a file; the `mut` definition presented the linker's derived-write-set check as what `mut` means, which is the definition a mission-critical reviewer reads to decide what `mut` proves, and it now says *linked* where it means linked; the `validate_extern` paragraph described a check a host import never reaches, naming `ConflictingHostSignature` and `ConflictingHostWriteSet` as the consistency check that stands in its place; and the module-resolution paragraph — the one a reader consults to answer whether `use { f } from host::io;` still finds `host/io.wasm` — presented its three search sources as what every name after `from` is resolved by, which is the one question the toolchain answers nowhere else and it answered "yes". `book/src/projects-and-the-infs-toolchain.md` records the new flag in its `infc` table and moves both ABI notes to `1.8` ([#464])
- The SpaceWasm chapter of `book/src/compilation_targets.md` gains a **Foreign modules** section and a **Conformance** section: what the build fails, what it reports, how to read each of the three numbers against `MAX_CONTROL_FRAMES` and `MAX_STACK_DEPTH` — with the unit each is in and why sizing either const generic from the other's number fails in flight — the over-budget warning, and the `wasm-opt` re-check. Its decode-limit table no longer says the check "lands in a later change": it gains the call-frame row, names the 31-byte host-registration cap beside the decoder's 32-byte name buffer, and says that a conformant module is one the interpreter will *decode*, and load once the embedder has registered hosts for whatever it imports — an import section is ordinary WebAssembly 1.0 and conformance is not instantiation. The new `core/target-conformance/README.md` carries every constant with the upstream file, line and **unit** it was transcribed from, the three-way split between decode limits, registration limits and measurements, and the shape of each message ([#325])

- The arithmetic-overflow surface is documented, and three sites the tree had already falsified are repaired in the same pass. `book/src/arithmetic-overflow-in-wasm-codegen.md` gains a *Checked and Wrapping Arithmetic* section — what an annotation governs and what it does not reach, the guard shapes with their measured byte costs, why the trap kind is a design constraint rather than an accident, the reproducer, what a proof can say about it, and a trap-attribution note. Two of its sections stop describing a compiler that does not exist: the "conceptual" rustc overflow-check snippet was not a valid sequence — after `local.tee $result` it pushed both operands and compared *them*, which is not an overflow test, and left the tee'd result stranded across the `if` — and is replaced by the same signed-addition predicate this compiler's own row uses; and *Overflow Checks as Proof Obligations* had proposed bare arithmetic in compile mode with an obligation generated in proof mode, which is exactly the mode divergence `target.rs`'s invariant forbids and `book/src/compilation_targets.md` contradicts in five places. *Overflow Checks in Non-Deterministic Blocks* described checks being "stripped from `spec` blocks and preserved in proof mode", a framing that predates the obligation translation — a `forall` or plain specification function has no compiled body to strip anything from — and now states the three rules that replaced the question. The `--overflow=trap` flag the chapter speculated about is gone: the arithmetic mode is a property of the language, not a build setting, and the chapter says so. `core/wasm-codegen/docs/specification-obligations.md` gains *No-Overflow as a Realization Claim*, with the emitted obligation quoted from the committed golden, the fact that the identical payload over an unmarked callee is unconditionally true, the both-bounds requirement and the asymmetry a neutralization run meets (widening a single bound by one still admits no overflowing pair), and the note that the relops carry the signedness the guard does not read. `book/src/unreachable-emission-in-codegen.md` now says which of the seven roles it is about, and its cost row no longer reads as the module's whole `unreachable` budget; `book/src/memory-allocation-in-wasm-codegen.md`'s restatement of the role count follows. `core/analysis/README.md` and `book/src/static-analysis.md` carry A052, A053 and A054, and the counts are re-derived from `all_rules()` rather than incremented — 51 rules, 45 error-severity and 6 warnings, with A013, A021 and A030 unassigned so the assigned ids run A001 to A054, and `book/src/the-language-server.md`'s code table follows. The linker README's account of the rejection is corrected to the widened form, and gains the obligation-symbol resolution the walk starts from. Two source doc comments stop stating a contract the tree no longer has: the narrow-division guard's, which said add/sub/mul wrap by policy, and the obligation translator's module doc, which claimed its terms mirror the operators code generation emits — true of every operator but the four an annotation governs, and the divergence is now written down beside the rules that fence it ([#316])
- Every in-tree statement that add, subtract and multiply wrap now says what they do instead, and the sites that had described the wrap as the *default* say which spelling asks for it. The arithmetic chapter's lowering examples carry the `wrapping(...)` that selects them, its language-comparison row moves to the trapping side and names the difference from Rust and Zig — no build profile takes the trap away — its constant-folding section is closed by A052 rather than open, its guard-cost figures are stated over the `wrapping(...)` spelling of the same source, and its reproducer traps as written. A paragraph beside them states the identity the figures rest on: an annotation and the default are two spellings of one thing, pinned by compiling one source under each fallback and comparing bytes. `core/wasm-to-v/ROCQ_CONTRACT.md`'s seventh `BI_unreachable` role now reads as what it is — a guard on every governed operator a program has not written inside a `wrapping(...)` — and its binary-operator table's claim to mirror code generation exactly gains the one exception it has: a term's `+`, `-` and `*` are the modular machine operators at every effective mode, so a compiled operator that traps has no counterpart there. `book/src/compilation_targets.md` records the guard among what both compilation modes emit. `book/src/memory-allocation-in-wasm-codegen.md`'s trap-role list drops a `checked(...)` qualifier the default made wrong, and its Zig comparison stops saying Inference emits no bounds checks — false since the guard began to ship in every build, and contradicted later on the same page. `book/src/the-wasm-linker.md` describes the `inference.checked` section beside the two obligation sections it already covered, and its error reference carries the two link failures the section drives. The linker and manifest documentation state the consequence a project meets rather than leaving it to be derived: a library with arithmetic in it carries the overflow-guard record, and one optimized under `[build.wasm-opt]` carries the opaque form, which no reachability specification may reach ([#316])
- `core/analysis/README.md` no longer lists **A013** as a rule of its own. Array-literal and struct-literal arguments were merged into A012 when the two checks became one, and the table had kept a row for the retired id, so the catalog claimed a rule `all_rules()` does not register and `rule_ids_match_diagnostic_rule_ids` cannot see. The row now says explicitly that A013 was merged into A012, which is what a reader following an old diagnostic needs. `book/src/static-analysis.md`'s rule count is recounted against `all_rules()` rather than restated — 48 rules, 43 of them error-severity and 5 warnings, with A013, A021 and A030 unassigned so the assigned ids run A001 to A051 — and `book/src/the-language-server.md`'s diagnostic-code table follows ([#360])
- Documentation that asserted a `todo!()` or a panic for a construct that now compiles, or that described a code generation abort as the outcome of a shape a rule now rejects, is corrected across `core/wasm-codegen/docs/`. `local-variables-lowering.md` no longer says array, string and unit literals "are not yet implemented and will produce a `todo!` panic" — arrays have lowered for months, unit is a no-op, and a string literal is an A048 diagnostic with a code generation backstop behind it. `type-checker-guarded-panics.md` is rewritten around the poison mechanism rather than around "codegen fails loudly instead": its first two inventory entries described compound-return sret sites that abort no longer, and the document now states what poison is, what invariant makes continuing safe, which sites are refusals, and which `assert!`/`panic!` invariants deliberately remain — the ones that hold no `CodegenError` and are bookkeeping claims about the compiler's own tables, such as a frame-layout entry that must exist. `assignment-lowering.md` and `function-calls-lowering.md` name the refusals their unsupported paths now produce instead of the aborts they used to, and `arrays-and-memory.md` corrects the sret-forwarding paragraph. `core/type-checker/docs/type-system.md`'s `string` section no longer offers `fn greet(name: string) -> string { return name; }` as a working example — it is two A048 errors — and its unit section records that unit is legal as a return type and rejected as a value type. `core/cli/src/main.rs`'s "no panics" claim is tied to the sweep that now enforces it ([#360])
- `ROCQ_CONTRACT.md` records the exact-artifact discharge floor as six cases, thirteen proved endpoints, one refuted and fourteen audited, with a table row for `linked-extern` and the reason that case is appended rather than ordered by shape. Two of its sections gain the corpus witness they had been asserting without one: "Obligations only an import-free module can discharge" names the two fixtures where a reachability obligation and a merge actually meet, since a reachability fixture that compiles alone can never reach the shape and a linked fixture with only universal obligations never needs the runtime; and "What a linked body brings that compiled code cannot" separates the three channels that reach a merged body — application, realization, and a reachability payload — because each is emitted by its own path, so a regression in one is invisible to the others. Both `ROCQ_CONTRACT.md` and `README.md` now state that the pinned verifier B certifies the first five cases only, that `linked-extern` is published ahead of its companion, and that the configured lane therefore fails closed on the six-case marker until the pin is bumped — the contract's A -> B -> C paragraph says a new selected artifact re-enters that sequence at A. `README.md`'s Docker-gate description follows the six ordered raw inputs and per-case bridge calls; its unrelated "five Cargo `test result:` lines" floor counts test binaries and is untouched ([#362])
- `book/src/the-wasm-linker.md` and `core/wasm-linker/README.md` no longer state that a linked library's proof obligations are "dropped, silently … with no way to opt a library's obligations into the main module's proof", and the README's claim that both `inference.*` sections are emitted only when the main module carried one is corrected for the adopting path. `ROCQ_CONTRACT.md` records how an adopted specification travels — an empty `inference.spec_funcs` index list, a namespaced key, and applied symbols rewritten onto the merged bodies — and notes that an adopted obligation is resolved twice, once in the library's own name section and once against the merged module. The linker's restatement of the translator's identifier rule is documented as **structural only**: the Rocq stdlib/keyword denylist is deliberately deferred to the translator, which rejects in the same `infc -v` invocation before any artifact is written, the reachable instances are named (`eq_refl`, `well_founded`), and a test pins in both directions that the denylist is the only rule deferred that way. The linker is stricter in one place of its own — it refuses a key ending in `_`, which the translator enforces downstream in a crate-private check — and the README names that too ([#362])
- The Rocq output contract now separates three claims that had been easy to conflate: vendored-stub elaboration, pinned real-library elaboration, and exact-artifact discharge for a named subset. The first two check interfaces, not truth, because normal generated theorem skeletons still end in `Admitted.`. The third requires fresh bytes equal to committed goldens, independent verifier-owned `Qed` endpoints over exact raw theorem types, and an assumption audit excluding raw and unapproved dependencies. Inference now pins wasm-verifier B (`181cd676662453182b9753d1b19ca933c68770c3`) and provides a separate protected CI job for that claim, using B's configured single-case adapter and an exact full-line success marker. It records the ordered five-case floor (bounded prime 2/0, exists 3/0, unique 3/0, narrow domain 2/0, false spec 1/1; eleven proved, one refuted, twelve audited endpoints) — superseded within this same unreleased section by the six-case floor recorded above — visibly reports an absent capability as `SKIPPED`, preserves the historical unbounded prime artifact as unchanged and uncertified, and completes the A -> B -> C reciprocal-pin bootstrap ([#450])
- `core/wasm-to-v/wasm-verifier-pin.txt` records the upstream revisions the emitted `.v` and the vendored stub are verified against — the wasm-verifier commit, the coq-wasm tag *and* the commit it resolves to, since a tag can be moved and a commit cannot — and is the single source the prose restatements in `ROCQ_CONTRACT.md` and `rocq-stub/README.md` are checked against, with an anti-vacuity floor so a scan that finds nothing fails instead of passing. It mirrors the conventions of wasm-verifier's reciprocal `book/inference-pin.txt` with one deliberate divergence: that file commits the cited source lines verbatim, which is safe recording a public repository inside a private one, while the reverse would publish private library source — so this file commits SHA-256 digests and counts, and the differing names are printed at failure time from the developer's own checkout. The previous pin had rotted 59 commits, was restated in prose in two places with nothing checking either, and named a commit that predates the reachability declarations it was supposed to cover ([#359])
- `rocq-stub/README.md` no longer prescribes deleting the stub once the real libraries are wired in, and no longer presents the six declarations [#401] removed as the stub-versus-upstream delta. Measured against the pin that delta is 32 declarations, eleven of them `term`/`hassert` constructors, so it is pinned as a digest rather than maintained by hand. The stub is kept deliberately: pointing `-Q` at the real `theories/` *widens* the name space, which loses the narrowing's guard — a module naming `HA_pto` is an unbound-constructor error against the stub and a clean pass against the real library — so the two gates are complementary rather than one superseding the other ([#359])

- Recorded the disposition of the pre-hassert proof-pipeline draft (PR #235, branch `feat/wasm-verifier-e2e`, closed unmerged and archived at tag `archive/pr-235-wasm-verifier-e2e`), whose three undecided pieces `ROCQ_CONTRACT.md` now settles in place rather than leaving to rot. **The obligation kind**: `inference.spec_funcs` stays at wire v1 and the section now says why a per-index kind byte was rejected there — a spec block may also hold methods, so a kind vector aligned with that index list is not aligned with the obligations it would describe, and an `exists`/`unique` entry additionally needs `entry_arity` and `visible_locs`, which a one-byte channel has no room for; the kind travels in `inference.hspecs` keyed by function symbol instead. **The `@`-to-parameter lowering**: a live concern, now met for every spec function the compiler emits, and recorded in a new section stating that the proof-mode artifact is standard WebAssembly, that the retained and omitted kinds need it for different reasons (only the retained one is a proof obligation), and that this is a property of how the artifact loads rather than of what it proves. **The `list assertion` `_specs` shape**: never released, so nothing migrates from it, but recorded under Migration because it is why wasm-verifier still guards against a generated `_specs` being rewritten to `list assertion` and why its assertion-payload predicate is named `ValidSpecFI`. Two stale rustdoc comments claiming spec indices are emitted as `list N` "consumed by the corresponding `ValidModule` theorems" are corrected — obligations have travelled as `hassert` payloads since the pipeline changed, and those indices now decide omission and renumbering, nothing more ([#364])
- Corrected the bounds-guard documentation against what the compiler does, after the proof-mode flip made several standing statements false. Three said proof mode emits no bounds check — `core/wasm-codegen/docs/specification-obligations.md`, `hassert/translate.rs`'s module doc, and `compiler.rs`'s narrow-division scratch field, which asserted by implication that the bounds scratch is still mode-gated; the specification-body range bound they surround is still a definedness rule, but because a specification body is never compiled, not because the mode is unguarded. "A constant index is guarded in no mode" was false as written: code generation folds a **direct number literal** and nothing else, so `a[K]` for a `const K` and `a[1 + 1]` are guarded, and the same narrowness leaves an out-of-range named constant in an executable function with no static rule at all — corrected in `ROCQ_CONTRACT.md`, `core/wasm-codegen/docs/arrays-and-memory.md` and `book/src/memory-allocation-in-wasm-codegen.md`, each of which now states the boundary and what falls on each side. The `emit_bounds_checks` flag's stated rationale was also wrong: [#215] elides per *access*, not per module, so the flag would stay `true` for every module under it; what keeps the flag is that `Compiler` is a library type whose in-crate tests exercise both settings, and `emit_index_offset` remains the seam a per-access elision hooks into. `book/src/memory-allocation-in-wasm-codegen.md` additionally attributed [#214]'s own "prove, then elide" step to [#215] and applied a `verified = deployed` caveat that does not fit it — removing a provably dead guard trades nothing away — and classified the narrow-division and `enum`-tag guards as cases where trapping is the mechanism, opposite to `ROCQ_CONTRACT.md`'s reading; both files now use one taxonomy, with `assert` and the reachability filter as the mechanism cases and the three guards as conditional traps whose condition is worth proving ([#214])
- `core/wasm-to-v/ROCQ_CONTRACT.md`'s trap-freedom section now records that `ValidSpec` is a **disjunction** — `ValidSpec_universal \/ exists rt, ValidSpec_at rt` — and which branch an emitted body can take. The section quoted the frame-universal branch alone, and `ValidSpec_at` appeared nowhere in the file, which is load-bearing rather than cosmetic: a body that owns a frame opens with the shadow-stack prologue and stores through the pointer it computes, nothing on the universal branch pins global 0, and so the left disjunct is refutable for every such body — including the new `spec_bounds_realization` fixture, whose theorem closes only through `ValidSpec_at`, as wasm-verifier's own `examples/E2ECallSpec.v` does with `right. exists e2e_call_runtime.`. The quoted `ValidSpec_universal` block also renamed the payload binder `hspec` to `h`, which reads as the abstract heap everywhere else in wasm-verifier; it is verbatim again ([#214])
- `core/wasm-to-v/rocq-stub/README.md`'s manual compile recipe never worked. Every line passed `-Q . Wasm`, which binds `wasm/bytes.v` to the logical name `Wasm.wasm.bytes`, so the first file carrying a cross-file import (`wasm/datatypes.v`, `From Wasm Require Import bytes`) failed with "Unable to locate library Wasm.bytes" — and because the two files ahead of it have no such import and compiled fine, the recipe looked half-finished rather than wrong. Each `-Q` now maps the physical directory to its logical name the way the `_CoqProject` excerpt two sections above already showed (`-Q wasm Wasm`, joined by `-Q wasm_verifier WasmVerifier` for the second namespace), and the section now records which artifacts a manual compile leaves beside the sources, that they are gitignored, and the `git clean -Xdf wasm wasm_verifier` that sweeps them — scoped to the two namespace directories, which hold nothing else ignored, rather than to the stub root. `book/src/projects-and-the-infs-toolchain.md`'s scaffolded-layout section gains the matching sentence for a user's own project: `coqc`'s outputs are ignored there too, and unanchored rather than confined to `proofs/` ([#437])
- Corrected `core/wasm-to-v`'s rejection documentation, which asserted properties the code did not have. `wasm_parser.rs`'s module doc claimed the parser "fails fast" on "out-of-bounds indices" (it performs no value-level validation at all, and still does not — a caller outside the linker pipeline owns that) and that component-model sections "are silently ignored" (they are now refused, as is the tag section, which the same file elsewhere called an error while the code ignored it). `README.md` and `ROCQ_CONTRACT.md` listed each unmodeled proposal family once, as though a family reached the module through a single surface; GC and stack switching reach it through **two** — an instruction and a type-section entry — and rejecting only the instruction is what left the type surface emitting a dangling `::`. Both rejection tables now split by surface, and note that any family added later needs the same question asked of it. `ROCQ_CONTRACT.md` also now states the `mod_types` positional invariant the emitter actually provides: one element per flattened type-section entry, in section order, so a position is the WASM type index by construction ([#365])
- `mut`'s write-set declaration is now documented as an affine over-approximation rather than a per-parameter address claim: `book/src/external-functions-and-wasm-linking.md`, `book/src/the-wasm-linker.md`, and `core/wasm-linker/README.md` each now note that a store computed from more than one parameter attributes to every parameter contributing a term, not only the one playing the role of address. The ordinary scaled-index write `mem[ptr + (idx << 2)] = val` attributes to both `ptr` and `idx`, so `external fn set_elem(ptr: i32, idx: i32, val: i32);` bound to a body that writes this way requires `mut` on `idx` as well as `ptr` — `set_elem(mut ptr: i32, mut idx: i32, val: i32);` — even though `idx` never itself denotes a location the body writes to; it only scales `ptr`'s. The read/write split (`memory.copy(dest, src, size)` records `src` as a `Load`, not a `Store`) keeps a pure source pointer correctly unforced — the over-attribution is specific to a store's own address computation, not to every parameter an instruction touches. This is not a behavior change: the affine attribution (`Linear::support`) has attributed this way since it shipped, and was previously undocumented ([#333])
- Documented #333's write-set contract, and fixed a rule-count drift the change exposed. `book/src/static-analysis.md`'s "forty rules… thirty-five error… five warnings" count was already stale before this change — three prior rules (A044, A045, A046) had shipped without updating it, and A044/A045 never appeared in the rule tables at all; recounted against `core/analysis/src/rules/mod.rs::all_rules()`, it is 44 rules (39 error, 5 warning) after this change, and A044/A045 are backfilled into their tables alongside new A047. `book/src/the-language-server.md`'s diagnostics table is corrected from `A001`…`A041` to `A001`…`A047`. `book/src/external-functions-and-wasm-linking.md` gains `mut`'s meaning, the unnamed-parameter-form deprecation for a writing external, and the scalar exemption as a documented restriction; `book/src/the-wasm-linker.md` gains the declared write-set check, its two `contracts` modes, and `LinkError::UndeclaredExternWrite`; `book/src/memory-allocation-in-wasm-codegen.md`'s copy-emission rule is rewritten to read the callee's own declaration rather than describing the future-work absence of one; `core/wasm-linker/README.md` documents the same check, the `attribution.rs` fixpoint, and re-points its #420 cross-reference now that #333 supplies a write-set channel narrower than containment; `core/wasm-to-v/ROCQ_CONTRACT.md` records that the write-set declaration is a link-time gate with no representation in the emitted `.v` ([#333])
- `core/wasm-linker/README.md`'s rejection policy re-checked row by row against `tier.rs::tier_c_reasons`, which had drifted twice in the same direction — describing a narrower envelope than the code accepts, and a laxer one than it enforces. The Tier-B admitted-form table still listed `p + p` as admitted by design, which the coefficient-parity lattice now rejects because doubling walks a pointer to a fixed absolute address rather than reaching further from it, and it omitted the scaled index `p + (q << 2)` admitted in the same change — the idiom every optimizing toolchain emits. The Tier C section and the classification table both described the three section- and table-derived signals as the whole story, when a memory access at an address provenance cannot trace back to a parameter is reported identically as `RequiresRelocatableBuild`; the table now shows classification as the two-step decision `tier::classify` actually makes. `core/wasm-to-v/ROCQ_CONTRACT.md` gains "What a linked body brings that compiled code cannot" — a merged body goes through the same lowering as a compiled one, and it is the linker's envelope rather than the translator that decides which foreign bodies arrive — records that `lim_max := None` is a memory shape only a link can produce, since code generation always emits a fixed memory with its minimum equal to its maximum, and corrects the standing claim that no corpus module can carry foreign bytes ([#363])
- `core/wasm-to-v/ROCQ_CONTRACT.md`'s "T_app resolution discipline" now states that an obligation symbol has **two** producers — code generation's mangled name and the linker's merged-body name — names `inference_fn_key::merged_name::root` as the shared one, records that an obligation about a linked external resolves only post-merge, and adds the arity step to the resolution list. `core/wasm-codegen/docs/specification-obligations.md`'s rejection table gains the matching **P005** row: the diagnostic now names an *unbound* extern callee rather than every extern callee, and a call that resolves to a function the module *links* is no longer rejected ([#363])
- New `core/wasm-codegen/docs/specification-obligations.md` for readers of an emitted `.v` — usually someone whose proof did not close: the two kinds of obligation and the asymmetry between them, aggregates as ordered scalar leaves with the enumeration and allocation rules that fix every `T_local` index in a goal, one fully expanded three-leaf obligation, the definedness reading of `a[i]` beside the both-bounds requirement a signed index carries, quantifier alternation, the caps, and a table of every kept rejection with its reason. `core/wasm-to-v/ROCQ_CONTRACT.md` gains the matching contract rows (aggregate introduction, aggregate copy, constant and non-constant access, leafwise aggregate comparison) and its **P001**–**P014** registry is brought current ([#355])
- Proof-mode diagnostics reworded now that the aggregate encoding makes several of them inaccurate. **P004**'s tail said "only bool, integer, and enum values can" at every site, which read as a rule the language no longer has — the parameter position accepts `[i32; 3]` and rejects `[Point; 2]`, and the reason is the shape rather than aggregation — so all its sites now name the representable surface exactly, and an aggregate read *whole* where a term is required (an aggregate call argument, most often) gets its own wording instead, since the shared one would have rejected `[i32; 2]` while listing arrays of integers as nameable. **P003**'s "is not supported" read as a schedule for a decision that is permanent, and now states the rule: a specification names values, not storage. `loop` is lifted out of the shared no-encoding template — the constructs sharing it have nothing to be rewritten *as*, while a loop's purpose in a specification is exactly what quantifying an index and constraining it says directly — and its message names that idiom. **P007**, **P008** and **P004** in an `exists`/`unique` body now name the quantifier that makes the construct impossible, because the identical declaration translates in a `forall` body, and they point at the `forall`-bodied alternative. Every message naming a quantifier takes the article that word is spoken with, which also corrects **P011**'s "an `unique`-quantified" ([#355])
- New `core/wasm-to-v/ROCQ_CONTRACT.md` documenting the external Rocq predicates (`ValidModule` 1-arg, new `ValidSpec`), the emitted proof-skeleton shape, and spec-map precedence rules ([issue#17])
- Rewrite `core/wasm-to-v/ROCQ_CONTRACT.md` for the wasm-verifier/vanilla-WasmCert target (hassert-valued `ValidSpec`, worked `.v` example, migration section); also rewrite `rocq-stub/README.md` and the `core/wasm-to-v/README.md` non-det section
- Add compilation targets matrix documentation (`book/compilation_targets.md`): Compile/Proof x Debug/Release x with/without non-det operations ([issue#97])
- Add `unreachable` emission rationale document (`book/unreachable-emission-in-codegen.md`) ([#144])
- Add arithmetic overflow in WASM codegen deep-dive (`book/arithmetic-overflow-in-wasm-codegen.md`) ([#146])
- Add a "mathcomp consumers" note to `core/wasm-to-v/ROCQ_CONTRACT.md`: emitted `.v` spells `N` literals with the `%N` scope key, which mathcomp's `ssrnat` rebinds to `nat_scope`, so a consumer that imports mathcomp ahead of the emitted definitions must re-delimit with `Local Delimit Scope N_scope with N.` after its mathcomp imports (`Local` because a file-global `Delimit` leaks to files that import the consumer); `%num` cannot be emitted instead without breaking the mathcomp-free standalone contract and the repo's `coqc` gate ([#413])

### Type Checker

- Three new binding diagnostics. `HostImportWithoutModule` refuses `use { … } from host;`, which stops at the reserved segment and names no import module, and `HostImportModuleNested` refuses `from host::a::b;`, since an import module string is one flat name an embedder registers rather than a path; each message says what a well-formed clause looks like and, for a linked module that used to be spelled that way, that the module has to be renamed instead. Both are raised once per *directive* rather than per imported field, and the directive's fields are left unbound rather than resolved against a module the clause never validly named, so neither refusal is followed by an `ExternImportNotDeclared` for each field it skipped, and the file's other, well-formed directives still bind. `ConflictingExternProvider` is checked across the whole program: one import module bound as a linked module by one clause and as a host import by another — in two files, or through two different fields of one file — is refused naming both clauses and both files, because an import module has one provider. The reservation that makes the first two reachable, and its migration, are the `### Breaking` entry ([#464])
- The head of a qualified call (`Type::method()`) is now read through parentheses and through a type application's base, so `Array::new()`, `Array u32'::new()`, `(Array u32')::new()` and `(Array)::new()` all reach one resolution and are refused alike with `method `new` not found on type `Array``. A head that read as no type name at all fell past method resolution into free-function handling, where the type checker accepted it and code generation refused it as a call to a proof-only specification function the program never declared — a message naming a construct that was not there, and on a valid cross-file `(Point)::make()` a refusal of a program that should compile. Parenthesization is not part of a callee's identity, and a type application resolves through its base because no type declaration in the language takes type arguments, so the arguments name nothing distinct to resolve against. Code generation's own head extractor peels parentheses too, so the two paths that derive one callee identity agree on the spellings that name a declaration. It stops short of the type application on purpose: A051 rejects one before code generation, and a head naming one has to reach a refusal rather than be lowered through its base, so that a caller skipping analysis is refused instead of served ([#320])
- New `DuplicateTypeParameterName` rejects a type-parameter name bound twice in one declaration (`fn g T' T'(x: i32)`), which parse, type check and analysis all admitted — only A051 reported it, as "generic over `T' T'`", which says nothing about the duplication. A repeat is not shadowing: an annotation resolves to the first binder, so the later one names nothing while still raising the declared arity, and every call site is then asked for a type argument no parameter position can supply. It sits beside the ordinary duplicate-parameter check, shares its wording shape and its caret on the repeat, and covers the free function, the struct method, the `spec` function and a method of a struct declared inside a `spec` through the one signature-validation arm every function-like declaration reaches ([#320])
- `CannotInferTypeParameter` no longer advises "consider adding explicit type arguments". A type argument is only ever inferred from the argument types at a call site — the grammar emits no type-argument field on a call, so the advice named a repair that cannot be typed. The message now names the two repairs that exist: declare a parameter of the type parameter's type so a call fixes it, or drop a binder nothing determines. `MissingTypeParameters` is gone with it — its only raise site fired on the same call at the same location as the per-parameter message, so one failure rendered twice, the second half implying arguments could have been supplied ([#320])
- Cross-file name resolution and file-based visibility for the module hierarchy: canonical file-qualified type identity, one `same_file` visibility chokepoint, per-call `CallTarget` records, and `CircularDefinition` detection for cross-file value cycles ([#63])
- Reject spec-inner functions whose bare name shadows a top-level function (`SpecFunctionShadowsTopLevel`); codegen and the type checker previously disagreed silently on which callee was invoked
- Reject same-named structs or enums across spec blocks at registration time (`RegistrationFailed`) instead of silently using the first-registered layout; functions remain mangleable across specs
- Spec blocks now open a real symbol-table scope via `enter_spec`, so two specs may declare same-named members without colliding ([issue#18])
- Remove `flatten_defs_with_spec_inner`; the three phases that used it recurse into `Def::Spec` inline, opening the spec scope around the inner work ([issue#18])
- `TypedContext::lookup_struct`/`lookup_enum` now search all scopes (`lookup_struct_anywhere`/`lookup_enum_anywhere`) so post-type-check phases can resolve spec-inner types ([issue#18])
- Add `resolve_custom_type()` to `SymbolTable`, resolving `TypeInfoKind::Custom(name)` to `Struct`/`Enum` at registration time and recursing into array element types ([#148])
- Add argument type validation at all function, method, and associated-function call sites ([#148])
- Add i64 array element type propagation from `[i64; N]` annotations to number literals in array initializers ([#148])
- Add array element and struct field assignment mutability checks (`arr[i] = value` and `p.x = 42` require `mut`), with `extract_root_variable_name` resolving the root identifier ([#148], [pr#159])
- Add `VariableShadowed` error: shadowing a name from an outer scope is a hard error, aligning with MISRA C Rule 5.3 and NASA Power of 10 ([pr#159])
- Add `ArrayReturnCallInExpressionPosition` error: sret calls permitted only in `let x = foo()` and `return foo()`, guarded at 6 sites ([#148])
- Add struct literal field validation: `MissingStructField`, `UnknownStructField`, `DuplicateStructField`, and field value type mismatches ([pr#159])
- Add `MethodNeverAccessesSelf` error: methods declaring `self` but never using it ([pr#159])
- Add `EmptyStruct` error: reject struct definitions with no fields or methods ([pr#159])
- Add `StructLiteralAsArgument` error: reject struct literals as direct function arguments ([pr#159])
- Add `CompoundLiteralInUnsupportedPosition` error: compound literals allowed only in variable declarations, assignments, returns, and struct field values ([pr#159])
- Extend `ArrayReturnCallInExpressionPosition` to also reject struct-returning calls in expression positions, including `MemberAccess` on sret calls ([pr#159])
- Add const initializer type validation: `const x: i32 = true;` now rejected ([pr#159])
- Add number-to-bool assignment rejection: `let x: bool = 0;` now rejected ([pr#159])
- Add ordering comparison validation: `true < false` now rejected; equality (`==`/`!=`) still allowed on all types ([pr#159])
- Fix duplicate `BinaryOperandTypeMismatch` error for mixed-type arithmetic ([pr#159])
- Remove dead code: `types_equal`, `is_compatible_with`, and `FuncInfo`'s `param_names` field
- Add `find_enclosing_variable_name()` to `TypedContext` for walking the AST parent chain to the enclosing variable
- Rename `ArrayReturnCallInExpressionPosition` to `CompoundReturnCallInExpressionPosition` to reflect struct coverage ([pr#178])
- Add `CompoundReturnCallInAssignment` error: `p = make_point()` rejected; use `let p = make_point()` instead ([pr#178])
- Add `MethodCallChainOnCompoundReturn` error: method call chains on compound-returning functions rejected — implicit temporaries cannot be named in formal proofs ([pr#178])
- Add `MethodMetadata` public struct and `TypedContext::lookup_method()` for cross-crate method metadata access ([pr#178])
- Migrate 13 codegen restriction checks from the type checker to the analysis pass; 11 `TypeCheckError` variants removed (46 remain, down from 50), `UzumakiInReassignment` and `ExternFunctionCall` are new
- Add 7 new `TypeCheckError` variants for validation hardening: `DuplicateStructFieldDefinition`, `RecursiveStructDefinition`, `InvalidAssignmentTarget`, `UninitializedVariable`, `ArrayLiteralSizeMismatch`, `DivisionByZero`, `DuplicateEnumVariant`
- Fix undeclared types in variable definitions now validated (previously missed in some positions)
- Fix case-insensitive type lookup removed — `I32` no longer resolves to `i32`; all type names are case-sensitive
- Fix `from_builtin_str` uses exact case-sensitive matching
- Fix external function parameter parsing in the AST builder (previously dropped parameters in some cases)
- Bump `tree-sitter-inference` grammar from 0.0.39 to 0.0.40 — fixes chained member access parsing
- Propagate `compound_literal_allowed` into nested struct literal fields and array literal elements, accepting `Outer { inner: Inner { x: 1 } }` and arrays inside struct fields ([pr#185])
- Add `find_enclosing_variable_name()` to `TypedContext` for analysis rule uzumaki struct name lookup ([pr#185])

### Testing

- **The Stellar host tier decodes every compiled contract's spec with the CLI's own reader and pins its bytes against `stellar-xdr`'s encoder.** A new module, `tests/tests/stellar/spec.rs`, takes every fixture of the compiled-contracts table and asserts that `soroban_spec::read::from_wasm` — the reader `stellar contract bindings rust --wasm` and the SDK's `contractimport!` run — returns its entries, each a `FunctionV0` whose name, input names, input types and outputs equal the export descriptor, with the source-to-spec type map written in the test rather than shared with the crate; the control is the default build and the Stellar target's code generation output before the rewrite, each `FromWasmError::NotFound`. The load-bearing check is an oracle: the expected section is built from the descriptor with the real `ScSpecEntry` types and written by `stellar-xdr`'s own encoder, and must equal the compiled section byte for byte, as `contractmetav0` must equal the encoding of its one `infver` entry, whose value is the crate's `CONTRACT_META_TOOLCHAIN_VERSION` rather than a spelled-out version. `add`'s 60-byte entry and `tick`'s 24-byte entry are pinned as bytes and annotated in `MEASURED_ABI.md`. `Spec::new` from `soroban-spec-tools` 28.0.0 decodes a contract's three sections — the environment metadata, the contract metadata and the spec — and fails the command on any one that does not decode; `stellar contract invoke` builds a call's arguments with it from the deployed contract, and `stellar contract info interface` runs it on a file. The tier mirrors `Spec::new`, decoding each section the same way, and the three are the last custom sections of every contract, in the order spec, meta, environment metadata. The two name bounds both gates refuse against, 32 bytes of method name and 30 of parameter name, are pinned to the widths of the `stellar-xdr` fields that hold the names: `MAX_INPUT_NAME_BYTES` and `MAX_EXPORT_NAME_BYTES` bytes fit an `ScSpecFunctionInputV0` name and an `ScSymbol` and one byte more does not, a contract with both names at the bound compiles and decodes, and one byte over either is refused by both gates, while a spec carrying such a name is refused whole by the reader. `envelope.rs` measures that the host reads neither tooling section: a contract carrying a well-formed spec and meta uploads and invokes, and so does one whose spec is seven `0xff` bytes or whose meta entry has a kind that does not exist. `soroban-spec = "=28.0.0"` is a dev-dependency of the tests crate, pinned exactly because it pins the `stellar-xdr =28.0.0` the host does; the `serde` feature it requires of `stellar-xdr` brings `serde_with` and its macro crates into the build ([#466])
- **The `stellar` CLI's flag alias is held against `heck` itself and against what the CLI measurably did.** A new module, `tests/tests/stellar/cli_flags.rs`, asserts that `inference_wasm_codegen::stellar_cli_flag_alias` equals `heck::ToKebabCase::to_kebab_case` on a table of 60 strings — underscores in every position, capitals alone, in runs and before a lowercase letter, digits between cases, and a few non-ASCII strings no identifier spells — and on every parameter name the fixtures under `tests/test_data/stellar` declare; that both gates' verdict on each of the six methods driven against `stellar` CLI 28.0.0 — `both(x, _x)` and five methods of one fixture — is admission exactly when every call through the flags `--help` lists succeeded; and that its table of those calls, and the two fixtures, are the ones `MEASURED_ABI.md` quotes. That chapter gains *Parameter names and the CLI's flag aliases*: the mechanism read from the CLI's source, the five-method run quoted verbatim, and a replica of the CLI's argument table parsed 400 times per call. `heck = "=0.5.0"` is a dev-dependency of the tests crate, pinned exactly to the version stellar-cli 28.0.0 locks — it has no dependencies of its own, and the pin stops resolving if another dependency later needs a newer 0.5.x, the moment to re-read the CLI's lock — and `ci/rocq-discharge.cargo-lock` lists it among the tests crate's dependencies, so the Rust 1.98 lane's `cargo fetch --locked` still passes ([#466])
- **The SpaceWasm embedder harness registers host-import stubs: `--host MODULE.FIELD=PARAMS[:RESULT]`**, repeatable, so an artifact binding `use { f } from host::<module>;` can be loaded and called by `cargo run -p inference-tests --example spacewasm-embed` instead of only being named as a load failure. The signature is spelled in the interpreter's own alphabet, one character per value type (`i` `i32`, `I` `i64`, `f` `f32`, `d` `f64`): `--host env.clock_ms=:I` is a nullary import returning an `i64`, `--host fprime_core.command=ii:i` takes two `i32` and returns one. A stub answers zero of its result type, never pauses or traps, never reads guest memory — a pointer argument is logged as a signed decimal, as a `NAME = value` result is, so an `i32` address at or above 2^31 prints negative — and prints each call to stderr as `host call: module.field(arg, …)`. The lines are held while the interpreter runs, because the interpreter owns a stub's `'static` closure and the stream a run reports to is only borrowed, and printed the moment it returns, before the result, trap or fuel line; a start function's calls are printed straight after the load. Every per-spec limit is asked of the interpreter's own constructors (`HostName::try_from_str`, `HostValList::try_new`, `HostFunction::try_new`) and refused as a usage error naming their error, the constant behind it and the spec's own length or count — a name over `HOST_MODULE_NAME_CAP` or `HOST_FUNCTION_NAME_CAP` bytes, more than `MAX_HOST_FUNCTION_PARAMS` parameters, more than one result — so the harness restates none of those, and a character that names no value type is named, a stray second `:` included; the stubs are built under the interpreter session, since `HostFunction::try_new` boxes through the interpreter's allocator, and before the module file is read. Specs are grouped into one host module per module name, wherever that name's specs stand on the command line, and the grouping is load-bearing in a way a two-stub run cannot show: 0.7.1's binder searches every host module carrying an import's module name, so a module per stub binds two stubs exactly as one module does, but the store addresses a host module through a `HostModuleRef` that silently wraps an index it cannot hold. Measured, a module per stub calls the 257th `env` stub as `env.f0`, and 257 distinct module names call `m256.f` as `m0.f` and exit 0 — so a command line naming more modules than a `HostModuleRef` addresses is a usage error stating both counts, the interpreter's read off its own `HostModuleRef` constructor. Grouping moves the same wrap inside one module, where nothing guards it either: the binder records a host function's position as `fi as u16` and the store never counts a module's functions, so, measured, 65,537 stubs under `env` bind an import of `env.f65536` to `env.f0` and exit 0 — so more than 65,536 stubs under one module name are a usage error too, a bound sized from the binder's sixteen-bit function index since no interpreter constructor judges it, and the duplicate check is a hash set so that such a command line parses in linear time. Two specs naming one import are a usage error whether they agree or not; the disagreeing pair is the hazard, since the binder takes the first host function of a matching name and a check keyed on the whole spec would register both. An export that re-exports a host import no longer reaches the shared `invoke`'s panic: it is reported as resolving to the host import `module.field`, with the reason — the interpreter invokes only functions with a WebAssembly body, and a host import's body is the embedder's — and the remedy, invoking an export that calls it, and exits `NO_SUCH_EXPORT`; the export listing a bare run or a missing export prints names it too, after the functions that can be invoked and with no signature to copy, as `ms: the host import clock.ms, which the interpreter cannot invoke`, rather than reading the export section as shorter than it is. The artifact is decoded under the empty module name: the interpreter refuses a module whose name a host module already carries, as `DuplicateModuleName` before it reads a section, and exempts only the empty name, so a guest named `main` would have refused every load at byte 8 beside `--host main.f=…` — and `use { f } from host::main;` is a binding this compiler accepts. A run without `--host` loads exactly as before, through the sweeps' own `decode` ([#464])
- The embedder harness's decode-failure report no longer ends by pointing a program that binds host imports at an open issue. Each import is listed beside what `--host` registered under its two names — `no stub; it needs --host env.clock_ms=:I`, `stub registered`, or, for a stub of another signature, what it gets wrong: `the stub --host env.clock_ms=i:I takes 1 parameter where the import takes none; it needs --host env.clock_ms=:I`, naming the half that differs and, for parameters of the wrong type, every position, grouped by the pair of types (`takes i64 as its ninth parameter where the import takes i32`; `takes i64 as its first and third parameters where the import takes i32, and takes f32 as its second parameter where the import takes i32`), since a clause naming only the first reads as the whole difference. The needed spec is derived from the import's own function type, read back through the option's parser and put to the same `host_stubs` a command line meets, so the report never prints a spec the interpreter would refuse — the interpreter decodes imports it can never bind, a tenth parameter or a 32-byte name among them. The suggestions are then put to `host_stubs` together, because a set can be refused where each spec alone is registered: a module importing one function from each of 257 module names is told `no --host set can supply them all`, with the host-module-count refusal, rather than handed 257 specs a command line would refuse.
