An import no spec can supply says why, and the reasons are kept apart because each has its own next step: it is a global, a memory or a table, which `--host` cannot register and an embedder's host module can; or its names cannot be written as `MODULE.FIELD` (a field containing a dot, an `=` in either name, an empty name), when they are printed apart as `module a, field b.c` beside what the obvious spelling would register instead or, for an `=`, beside the name it would end, since `--host` splits a spec at its first `=`. An import the interpreter cannot load however it is supplied is a kind of its own, since an embedder is no remedy either: its type holds `externref`, `v128` or any other value type outside the four `--host`'s letters spell, which are the interpreter's whole value-type set (0.7.1 refuses anything else as `MalformedValueType`); it is a tag import, which its import reader refuses as `MalformedImportExportDesc`; its function type cannot be read; an earlier import has the same two names and another type — WebAssembly allows the pair, the interpreter binds a name to the first function registered under it, and a second spec for the same names is one `--host` refuses as given twice, so none is suggested; or the interpreter refuses the host function it would bind to — a name over 31 bytes, a tenth parameter, a second result — quoted as `--host` itself would refuse that spec, because every embedder builds a host function through the same constructors and their types hold no more. A spec naming no import of the module is listed after the imports as `--host env.clok_ms=:I names no import of this module`, so a misspelt name is read off the report rather than found by comparing it against the command line. The two verdicts were measured: an import with no stub is `FunctionImportNotFound`, one whose stub has another signature is the distinct `FunctionImportTypeMismatch`, and neither names the import, which is why the harness compares each stub with its import itself. The closing line is chosen so that it never offers a fix that cannot work, in this order: `no SpaceWasm embedder can load this module as built` when some import is one the interpreter cannot load; `the interpreter refused the module before it bound any import, so no stub changes this verdict` when the decoder stopped outside the import section while some import is still unsupplied, which it can do only before reaching that section — a `v128` function type no import uses is refused in the type section, and the stub the report lists for the import beside it would meet the same verdict; `run the module from the embedder that supplies them` when no `--host` set can supply the suggestions together; `--host can stub only some of these, so run the module from the embedder that supplies them` when imports a stub can supply sit beside imports only an embedder can, since a reader who added the stubs would otherwise meet a second refusal before the pointer they needed; `run the module from the embedder that supplies them` when every import left unsupplied is one no spec can supply; and `register a stub for each with --host MODULE.FIELD=PARAMS[:RESULT], or run the module from the embedder that supplies them` only when stubs are all that is missing. A module refused for its code is not told to register stubs it already has, and a reader is not told to register a stub the line above says cannot exist ([#464])
- `tests/tests/spacewasm/host_imports.rs` runs a compiled program against the host functions it imports, in process under the real interpreter: the F´ fixture `host_import_fprime.inf`, which binds all six hosts of the F´ (F Prime) reference set at the signatures `spacewasm_std` registers them with — `fprime_core.panic(i32, i32, i32)`, `rsleep(i64)`, `command(i32, i32) -> i32`, `message(i32, i32)`, `telemetry(i32, i32, i32, i32, i32) -> i32` and `env.clock_ms() -> i64`, an array argument being one `i32` address — compiled once at `Target::SpaceWasm`, decoded against recording hosts registered at those signatures that answer `command` with 70,007, `telemetry` with 0 and `clock_ms` with 1,700,000,000,123. `report(3)` returns `1700000000123`, and the recording is exactly `message("report", 6)`, `command(1, 3)`, `telemetry(70007, [1, 2, …, 11], 11, [21, 0, 0, 0], 4)`, `clock_ms()` — the 70,007 is `command`'s answer, threaded into `telemetry`'s `id` by the program. A recording host reads each array argument through the address it was handed and the length after it and records the bytes, never the address, which is where the compiler's frame layout put the buffer rather than anything the program says. The program seeds `time` with the bytes 1 to 11 rather than zeros, since an address into any untouched stretch of the guest's memory would read eleven zeros too — measured by having the recording host read `time` from address 0, which passed the row while the buffer was zeros and fails it now that it is seeded. Each answer is wider than a narrowing would keep: the interpreter's stack holds an `i64` as two 32-bit words, and a result of which only the low word reached the guest comes back as another number, where a reading under 2^32 survives unchanged — measured by narrowing the result to its low word in the harness, which fails this row and passes it with a clock reading of 123,456 — and an acknowledgement above 65,535 is likewise changed by a cut to sixteen bits. Measured by neutralization: a program passing `channel` as `telemetry`'s `id` instead of the acknowledgement leaves the result at the clock reading, so it is the whole-recording comparison, not the result, that proves the values crossed the boundary. Beside it: refused telemetry (answering 1) returns 0 without calling the clock; no hosts is `FunctionImportNotFound`; each of the six hosts registered with a parameter too many or another result type, and each of the five that take parameters registered with its first parameter at the other integer type, is `FunctionImportTypeMismatch`, seventeen refusals, since the program binds all six — `panic` and `rsleep` among them, though it never calls them; `spacewasm::check` accepts the same bytes; and a host that traps makes the call return `Outcome::Trap(TrapReason::Host)` rather than panic the harness. The CLI matrix gains thirty-two rows over `--host`, from the F´ program compiled from source against a stub for each of the six reference hosts (`report = 0`, with `message`, `command(1, 3)`, `telemetry(0, …)` and `clock_ms()` logged, every array argument matched as whatever address the frame layout gave it — a matcher a thirty-third row pins) and a program calling all six, to the form and cap refusals on both sides of each cap, the order between a host call and the result read from both streams interleaved, a host call ahead of a trap and ahead of an exhausted budget, a start function's call printed straight after the load — ahead of the `loaded` line, the measurement, and a missing-export or argument refusal — and once ahead of the invocation's, the two sides of both the host-module count and the per-module function count, one module's specs split around 255 other module names on both sides of the count, a program built from `use { f } from host::main;`, and one row per closing line of the decode-failure report ([#464], [#485])
- `support::host_module(name, functions, globals)` and `support::host_set(modules)` build every host module (and so every non-empty host set) in the SpaceWasm tier: the conformance oracle's `host_exporting` and `host_with_global`, which each spelled out the whole `HostModule` literal and its one-element vector, the host-imports tier's recording hosts, and the `--host` stubs. The seam is the module rather than the one-element vector because `--host` and the host-imports tier register several modules at once. `host_module` hands back the interpreter's `HostNameError` rather than panicking, since `--host` passes it a name somebody typed; the oracle's helpers still `expect`, as their rows mean them to. Because the `--host` path reaches both, they are live in all three binaries that compile the shared support file without a third `#[allow(dead_code)]` ([#464])
- A SpaceWasm module can now be run from a command line: `cargo run -p inference-tests --example spacewasm-embed -- out/main.wasm --invoke main`. `spacewasm` is a library with no front end of its own, so until now a finished artifact could only be exercised from inside a sweep. The harness loads a module under the reference embedder configuration, calls one export with decimal arguments coerced to the parameter types the *artifact* declares, and prints `NAME = value` or `NAME = (unit)`; with neither `--invoke` nor `--stats` it lists what the module exports, which is the cheapest way to ask whether an artifact loads at all, a measurement being the report of the run that asked for one. Each way a run can end has an exit code of its own — unreadable file, refused bytes, missing export, trap, exhausted fuel — because "it did not work" is the one thing an integrator already knows, and `--fuel N` bounds execution so a non-terminating program fails a run instead of hanging it. A module whose imports nothing supplies is a load failure that names each `module.field`, which the interpreter's own verdict cannot: a flight decoder keeps no string it does not have to, so the module is read a second time for the names. `--stats` reports the IR pages, the sixteen-bit words written into them, those words as bytes, the artifact's size and the ratio of the last two, with `--json` printing the same measurement as one line — always the first line of stdout, so a reader takes line one and has the whole thing even when `--invoke` prints beneath it. Pages and words come out of upstream's own two formulas and are comparable with `spacewasm_std`'s output line for line; the ratio is reported as `IR bytes per wasm byte` and is deliberately **not** given the name upstream gives its own *compilation ratio*, which divides the live bytes its bounded page allocator holds — engine and guest memory as much as IR — and which this harness cannot produce, because the tier runs on an unbounded allocator that keeps no statistics. A figure that is not upstream's must not reach a log under upstream's name, least of all as a JSON key, which travels without the sentence that would have explained it. Benchmark *tracking* is not here; the JSON line is the hook. It is an **example** rather than a `src/bin` target so that it sees `spacewasm`, which only a test, an example or a bench does, and its body is three lines over a `run` that lives in the tier's shared support module — which is what lets `tests/tests/spacewasm_embed_cli.rs` drive its whole matrix through it **in process** rather than shelling out to `cargo run` from inside `cargo test` and contending for the build lock the run is already holding. Every row states in its own words what would make it fail ([#325])
- The conformance checker is held to the runtime it describes rather than to its own transcription. `tests/tests/spacewasm/conformance_oracle.rs` puts a hand-written module on **both** sides of every boundary, in front of `check` and in front of the real interpreter, and requires the two verdicts to be equal: 255 parameter words against 256, and 128 `i64` parameters to pin that it is a *word* limit; the local-word and call-frame bounds at 65,533 / 65,534 / 65,536; a 65,536-local group; import names at 31, 32 and 33 bytes, where the accepting row registers a real host module and the refusing rows assert that `HostName<31>` cannot be built for the name at all; host arity at 9 and 10; a two-result import; custom section names at 32 and 33; a 65,537-page memory; and `memory.copy`, `i32.extend8_s` and a raw `0xfc 0x3a`. The two verifier bounds are pinned by decoding twice, at the reported number and one below it, which is what makes `MAX_STACK_DEPTH`'s unit a measurement rather than a comment: sixteen live `i32` values and sixteen live `i64` values both report sixteen *values* and 16 against 32 *words*, and both decode at a value stack of exactly sixteen and fail at fifteen. The two limits on the interpreter's compiled IR are pinned there too, and their rows assert the decoder's *reason* rather than only its verdict, since every module reaching them is large or deep and a refusal for a page budget would otherwise satisfy the row while saying nothing: a `call_indirect` against type 65,535 of 65,536 loads and against 65,536 of 65,537 is `IdxTooLarge`, which is why that instruction is the boundary rather than the `br_table` beside it — a table of 65,535 targets is inside the immediate and costs 131,070 IR words, so the accepting side of *that* boundary is out of reach of any plausible embedder and the row says so instead of pretending otherwise. The unwind rows are 255 against 256 `i32`, 127 against 128 `i64` — the pair that makes it a word limit — and 127 `i64` plus one and plus two `i32`, which is the only shape that separates a word sum from an operand count weighted at the width of the first. Three further rows exist because a boundary alone would not have caught the model being permissive: a `br_if` standing on 255 operands *and* its condition is accepted, so a model that forgot the selector comes off first would refuse a module the decoder loads; a `br_table` whose listed target is shallow and whose **default** is deep is refused, so a model measuring "the targets" would miss it; and 256 operands live across a `br` to the function's own label, and across a `return`, are both loaded, so a model measuring every branch alike would refuse a program that runs. Beside it, `tests/src/codegen/wasm/spacewasm_conformance.rs` commits every default-partition golden's exact control depth, operand height and operand width in a reviewed table with a totality gate and a count floor, so a lowering that deepens nesting shows up as a diff on a pull request rather than as a load failure on hardware — "no worse than before" would have permitted exactly the wrong direction. The two tiers disagree about one class, deliberately and with a test that says so: an import-bearing golden is conformant WebAssembly 1.0 and the decode tier still refuses it, because conformance is a property of the bytes and instantiation needs a host module that tier registers none of. The oracle itself has one class where `check` and the decoder disagree on purpose, and its rows state **both** verdicts rather than requiring one: a reference to a definition at 65,536 or beyond is refused here and *loaded* there, so each row also runs the loaded module and records which definition it actually reaches — `global.get 65536` answers global 0's value, `global.set 65536` lands on global 0, `call 65536` runs definition 0, an export of function 65,536 resolves to definition 0 for the life of the module, an element entry of 65,536 fills the table slot with definition 0, and a `start` section naming 65,536 runs definition 0 before an embedder calls anything. Every row carries the 65,535 neighbour that both sides accept and run correctly, because a checker that refused every large module would satisfy a boundary alone, one row pairs the same written index against a module with one imported global to pin that the position is the index minus the imported count and not the index, and one carries two element segments with the over-cap entry in the second, since a single-segment fixture cannot tell a counted segment index from a constant. The `call` row measures the `spacewasm_std` code-page budget refusing 65,537 bodies with `AllocError(OutOfMemory)` in place, and measures the smallest budget that does hold them, before the four function-index rows decode under the widened `CompilerOptions::max_code_pages` it establishes, so that widening is a stated step rather than a silent one ([#325])

- The `spacewasm` target is measured against the **real SpaceWasm flight interpreter**, in process, by two sweeps in a new integration binary at `tests/tests/spacewasm/`. **`decode_sweep`** puts the committed golden corpus in front of the decoder and requires the two partitions to land on opposite sides of it: the default partition's goldens decode, validate and IR-compile under `Module::new::<64, 256>` — `spacewasm_std`'s own configuration, pinned by a test of its own, since moving either bound re-measures every claim the tier makes — and the opt-in `bulk_memory_golden` goldens are refused with `InvalidOpcode(0xfc)` raised in the code section, which is what makes the target's post-MVP refusal a measured property of the runtime rather than a claim about it. Asserting merely "this failed" would be satisfied by a size or depth limit and would keep passing on the day the decoder grew bulk memory, which is exactly the day the refusal in `codegen()` should be revisited. Two classes of default-partition golden are excluded from the decoding claim, and neither is a SpaceWasm verdict on the compiler: the fifteen that declare an import, which resolves against a host-module set this tier leaves empty, and the sixteen that carry the compiler's custom `0xfc` verification operators, which are not WebAssembly at all. Membership is decided by reading the module rather than by naming files, and each class is then compared against a **committed** list of the goldens in it: a classifier compared against itself agrees whatever the corpus does, so it is the committed list that makes a fixture growing an import fail here instead of quietly shrinking what the sweep covers. Both classes are then put in front of the decoder anyway, because a skip is where coverage goes to die — an import-bearing golden has to come back with an *unresolved import*, an operator-bearing one with `InvalidOpcode(0xfc)` in the code section, which is the tier's statement that a module built for the proof toolchain does not fly. The same rule covers the opt-in partition's own excluded class: the seven goldens carrying a verification operator as well are decoded and refused like the rest, and are only kept out of the bulk-memory *count*, since their `0xfc` would be evidence about the proof toolchain rather than about bulk memory. **`differential`** stops asking whether a module loads and asks whether it computes the same thing: each fixture is compiled once, at the `spacewasm` target, and those exact bytes are run under both `wasmtime` and the flight interpreter — every exported zero-parameter function of every import-free one, with the two engines required to agree on the returned value and on whether the call trapped. Compiling twice would measure byte identity, which the corpus-wide identity sweep already owns; running one module under two independent implementations measures the only thing left. The unit of coverage is an invocation and not a fixture: filtering on a `main` would have covered a handful of fixtures and read in a summary as though it covered the corpus, so `main` is one row among the rest rather than the gate. Zero-parameter is read off the *WebAssembly* signature and not off the source, since a lowering that returns an aggregate takes a hidden pointer the source never declared, and per fixture the compiler's export descriptor and the flight decoder's export list must name the same set — two descriptions of one module, required to agree. The three dispositions that keep a fixture out of the comparison — refused by the target's envelope, emitting a verification operator, emitting an import — are each compared against a committed list of the fixtures in them, so a fixture leaving the compared set goes red instead of being absorbed by the headroom under a count floor; a fixture the envelope refuses has to say so in its message as well, or one that stopped compiling for an unrelated reason would leave the compared set in silence. The floors stay underneath the equalities as the vacuity backstop, and they are what the numbers in this entry are: at least 100 goldens decoding, at least 50 bulk-memory refusals, at least 270 invocations over at least 100 fixtures, at least 10 envelope refusals, at least one trapping row and at least one `main`. A **committed** negative control runs both readers over modules that genuinely differ, in both of the ways the sweep compares — a different returned value, and a trap where the other side returned — because every assertion in the sweep is an equality and an equality between two constants is satisfied by a harness that reads nothing; it is committed rather than demonstrated by a one-off neutralization, whose evidence dies with the review comment. Trap parity gets a program written for it as well, since the trapping half of `arith_overflow` takes its operands as **parameters** and a zero-parameter sweep barely reaches it. The spec-bearing row is inline for a reason of the same kind — every spec-bearing codegen fixture in the tree lives under a multi-file `src` directory the single-file walk skips — and requires the compile-mode module to decode, to run, to export the shipped half and nothing else, and to carry no `0xfc` operator at all ([#325])
- The SpaceWasm tier's harness is a pinned dev-dependency and a session token. `spacewasm = "=0.7.1"` with `strict-assertions` — upstream's own dev-dependency setting, which re-checks at run time the bounds the verifier established and so can only make the tier stricter — is a dev-dependency of the tests crate, so neither `cargo build` nor a `cargo clippy` without `--all-targets` compiles any of it: measured, a `cargo clean -p spacewasm` followed by a `cargo build` of the default members produces no `libspacewasm` rlib, and the crate appears in the package's dev-dependency tree and not in its normal one. The `=`-pin is the point rather than caution, since the decoder's acceptance set is what this tier records, so a version bump has to be a reviewed act with both sweeps re-measured; it adds exactly one package to the graph, `libm 0.2.16` being already in it. `spacewasm::global_allocator!` expands to `static mut` globals and two `no_mangle` symbols, and upstream documents those entry points as single-threaded and non-re-entrant, putting the synchronization on the embedder: every route to the interpreter therefore runs through an unforgeable `SpaceWasmSession` token, and a `LoadedModule` *borrows* the session it was decoded under, so it cannot outlive the lock its memory is freed through. With a zero-sized allocator it is that contract the token keeps rather than an observed write race — and it stops being only a contract the moment the allocator carries state, which the bounded page allocator a flight embedder ships would give it. The macro is invoked at the binary root and never in the shared harness file, so the `static mut` cannot reach the tests library or its `rocq-discharge` binaries. Two facts about the runtime are now recorded by tests rather than by prose: exceeding `MAX_CONTROL_FRAMES` surfaces as `AllocError(OutOfMemory)` from the verifier's fixed-capacity frame stack and never as `ControlFlowTooDeep`, which `spacewasm` 0.7.1 declares, maps in its C API and never constructs; and the target's non-determinism gate, while still a backstop rather than the diagnostic, is now a decision within a body as well as over definitions, so a corpus sweep with analysis skipped reaches **no** fixture whose module carries a verification operator: the differential sweep's committed `OPERATOR_EMITTING` list is empty and its emptiness is the claim, the fixture whose `forall` sits in a loop body having moved into the envelope-refused list beside it. `ci/rocq-discharge.cargo-lock` is regenerated in the same change, since the tracked lane lock records `inference-tests`' own dependency array and any addition to it — a dev-dependency included — makes the lane's `cargo +1.98.0 fetch --locked` abort on `push: main`; the regenerated lock adds that one package and nothing else, since a full re-resolution moves crates this change has no opinion about ([#325])
- The two gates that state the Stellar admissibility policy are checked against each other by a new integration target at `tests/tests/stellar_abi_parity.rs`. The policy — how many parameters a contract method takes, how long its name may be, which prefix is reserved, which types may cross the boundary — is stated once in `inference-wasm-codegen`, over the program the author wrote, and again in `inference-stellar-abi`, over the linked module and its export descriptor. The duplication is structural and stays: the dependency runs from the rewriter to the code generator, so neither can read the other's constants. Nothing compared them, and each crate's own boundary tests are written in terms of its own constant — so editing a constant moved its own boundary test with it and both suites stayed green through the drift, which is to say they could not individually notice that one had moved. A table of rows now puts one program in front of both gates and requires the same answer. A row the source gate refuses still reaches the rewriter, by being built for the default target: that skips the gate and yields the same export descriptor, which is populated off the source declarations at every target. Each row additionally pins what the policy admits, because parity on its own is satisfied by two gates that check nothing — delete both and every row agrees. Rewriter refusals are matched against an allow-list of the admissibility variants, so a row refused over the declared protocol, the WebAssembly 1.0 feature set or a descriptor mismatch goes red rather than agreeing for a reason the policy never mentions. Two rules stay unit-tested inside each crate: an empty export name and a name outside `[A-Za-z0-9_]` are not expressible in source, because an export name is an identifier ([#324])
- The Stellar target is measured against the **real Soroban contract host**, in process, by a new integration binary at `tests/tests/stellar/`. `soroban-env-host = "=28.0.2"` with `testutils` is a dev-dependency of the tests crate, so neither `cargo build` nor a `cargo clippy` without `--all-targets` compiles any of it; the host is chosen over `soroban-sdk` because it is Apache-2.0, accepts raw `&[u8]` module bytes, sits under this workspace's MSRV and writes no snapshot files. Nothing talks to a network. Three tiers. **`envelope`** pins what the host accepts of a module's *shape*: the `contractenvmetav0` section as its exact 32 bytes rather than as a length, protocol 20 accepted and 99 refused, floats, multi-value, an exported mutable global, an export name at exactly 32 bytes and at 33, a `__` prefix, a hyphen, two exports sharing a name, a memory exported under a name other than `memory`, and a data segment past the declared initial memory. **`wrappers`** assembles each `Val` marshalling sequence with `wasm-encoder`, executes it on the host and round-trips it, and pins all seven as bytes; its `Val` codec re-derives the published bit layout instead of calling the toolchain code it exists to check, since a harness sharing an implementation with its subject can only prove the two agree. **`contracts`** leaves hand assembly behind: thirteen `.inf` fixtures under `tests/test_data/stellar/` are compiled through code generation at the Stellar target, the link and the Val-ABI rewrite — the three steps `infc --target stellar` runs, reached through a new `pub mod corpus` seam in the tests library, since an integration binary cannot see a `pub(crate)` helper — then uploaded and invoked, twenty-three invocations in all. Every answer is also compared against the *same source built for the default target* under `wasmtime`: the values must agree, which is what says the wrapper adds marshalling and nothing else, and what makes "prove the default build, deploy the Stellar one" a claim about the deployed artifact. Coverage is held in step by two guards, at file granularity and at method granularity, because a method added to a fixture that already has a case is otherwise wrapped, exported and invoked by nothing. One fixture's body forces linear memory, so the upload, the export retarget over non-function entries and the invocation are measured on a contract carrying a memory section, a `memory` export and a `__stack_pointer` export rather than only on the scalar-only shape; one puts a private function ahead of its exported methods so exported indices are neither zero-based nor contiguous; one carries `main`, the export the emitter reaches through a branch of its own. Every negative is paired with the control that differs from it in exactly one property, because the host is the code under test and cannot be neutralized. The whole record — every sequence's WAT and bytes, the host's verbatim error text, the export-identity rules at the stage they actually bite, and nine corrections to the written ABI description — is `tests/tests/stellar/MEASURED_ABI.md`, whose byte columns and coverage counts are pinned by tests so it cannot go stale silently ([#324])
- The `arith_overflow` fixture now carries its eight boundary operations twice, and the two halves assert opposite things. The `const`-driven functions are written `wrapping(...)` and keep the exact eight values the fixture asserted before overflow began to trap; eight new functions take the same operands as **parameters**, unmarked, and the execution test calls each on the vector its twin wraps at and asserts the trap is the guard's own `unreachable` rather than anything the machine raises. Parameters are not a stylistic choice: the four-tier golden path runs analysis, and a constant that leaves its type is now refused before code generation, so the trapping half could not have been written from `const` bindings at all. The golden pins the split as well — eight annotations, eight guards, and none of the guards in an annotated twin ([#316])
- Five test surfaces cover the overflow-guard work, and each is there because the one beside it cannot see what it sees. **`checked_arith`** is a four-tier golden — bytes, WAT, stock-feature validation, Wasmtime execution — over a fixture carrying one exported function per catalogue row at every width and signedness, plus the fixed-point reproducer, nested annotations of the same and of opposite modes, a function holding both width classes, and a guard inside a loop with a `break` after it. Its execution matrix asserts the trap **kind** rather than merely that a trap happened, which is the assertion that has teeth: delete the signed multiply's `b == -1` arm and `(MIN, -1)` still traps, through WebAssembly's own `integer overflow` instead of through `unreachable`, and only a kind assertion goes red. The matrix also pins the vectors that must *not* trap, `2^62 * -2` landing exactly on `i64::MIN` among them, and the same source compiled in both modes is byte-identical. **`spec_overflow_realization`** joins the `coqc` corpus with a committed `.v` twin, and its needles are fixture-unique: the four nested `BI_if (BT_valtype None)` of the division round-trip counted exactly, each of the round-trip's four load-bearing conditionals matched as a sequence, two signed divisions and no more (the guard's and the source's own `/ ONE`), and the narrow companion's re-narrow-and-compare shape asserted as one conditional rather than a nest. That gate establishes that the emitted `.v` *elaborates* and cannot establish that either obligation is **true** — generated skeletons end in `Admitted.`, so a false obligation compiles as readily as a true one — which the fixture's own comment says, naming where truth is established instead: `theories/examples/Issue316OverflowExample.v` against the real library. The fixture also takes a `stock_validity` row, as the only one whose executable bodies carry an overflow guard. **`panic_free`** gains an `arith_modes` shape fixture putting both annotations in every position that lowers one — a free function's return expression, a method body, a comparison whose operands are sums, one annotation nested inside another, and a `const` initializer — since each is a separate lowering path rather than a repetition of one. **`checked_link`** covers the section end to end across twenty-three cases: what a library with and without guards carries, the merged module's post-link indices, `forall` linking and `exists`/`unique` being refused one hop, two hops and through the program's own function, a merged artifact linked as a library by a *second* link still reporting the guard it absorbed, the opaque form under each specification kind, an obligation resolving to no function, a malformed or duplicated section refused at parse time, the proof translation reading nothing from it, and the two crates' copies of the section name and version agreeing. And the corpus audit in **`tests/src/rocq_corpus_invariants.rs`** is load-bearing rather than latent: it counts the applications it examined and fails on zero, and removing one operand's envelope from the overflow fixture makes it name the fixture, the specification, the callee and the slot ([#316])
- Eight golden regenerators could not have produced the goldens they name, and the flip is the first change that had to run them all. Five of them — `binops_compound`, `binops_i64_bitwise`, `binops_paren`, `binops_shift_edge` and `binops_u32` — built their fixture directory by joining the fixture name twice, so every read and write went to a path no fixture occupies and the helper panicked before it emitted anything. The other three — `base::regenerate_if_nondet_wasm`, `loops::regenerate_loop_in_nondet` and `loops::regenerate_nondet_then_break` — produced their bytes with the analysing compiler while the assertion beside them compares against the non-analysing one, so a golden written by the helper would have failed the test the helper exists to feed. Both faults are silent while the goldens hold still, which is why a flip that moves 241 of them is what surfaced them ([#316])
- The fresh-export test in `tests/src/rocq_dischargeability/mod.rs` listed five expected raw SHA-256 hashes against six cases and zipped the two together, and `zip` stops at the shorter side: the sixth case's bytes were never hash-checked by the test whose whole purpose is to hash-check them, and the case it dropped was `linked-extern`, the one artifact assembled by the linker rather than emitted by one compilation. The list now carries one hash per case, and the sibling test's hardcoded raw-file count reads `CASES.len()` so neither can fall behind the table again ([#316])
- The selected-artifact discharge gate has a seventh case. `overflow` publishes `tests/test_data/rocq/spec_overflow_realization.v`, the only selected artifact whose executable bodies carry an overflow guard, so it is the only endpoint certified over arithmetic that can trap rather than over arithmetic that cannot. The aggregate floor moves from six cases, thirteen proved and one refuted to seven, fifteen and one, and the audited-endpoint ceiling from fourteen to sixteen, everywhere either is stated: the request, the receipt validator, the success marker, the protected CI job and the summary it writes, and the Docker adapter's ordered raw and receipt sets, whose six-way unrolled fingerprint, staging, copy and identity checks each gain the seventh entry — as does the fake-Docker self-test that freezes that contract, which passes only when the adapter makes exactly seven per-case bridge calls in the table's order. `README.md` and `core/wasm-to-v/ROCQ_CONTRACT.md` carry the new floor and place `overflow` at the A of its own A -> B -> C round, so the configured lane fails closed on the seven-case marker until the pin names a verifier that proves it ([#316])
- A new **`panic_free` sweep** (`tests/src/panic_free.rs`) enforces the property this work exists to establish: no program the front end accepts may abort code generation. It runs parse, type checking, analysis and code generation inside `catch_unwind` over every fixture in the repository, in **both** compilation modes — 218 fixtures and 436 compilations — and reports an abort as a named failure. Both modes are required, not thorough: compile mode never runs choice lowering or the obligation translation, and those hold their own accounting of what a declared parameter costs, so a disagreement about an unnamed parameter's slot would ship green under a compile-only sweep. Two of the three sources are directory walks, so a fixture added anywhere joins the sweep without anyone remembering to list it: the 49-file language corpus, and the 147 canonical paired golden fixtures selected by the rule that a fixture's file stem equals its parent directory name (the 22 files that rule excludes are exactly the multi-file project trees, whose `use` clauses need a project driver this in-process pipeline does not have). The third is a hand-written table of 22 single-offence programs under `tests/test_data/panic_free/`, one per construct that had no lowering, each declaring the stage it stops at. Four gates run over that: no fixture panics; every declared shape reaches its declared verdict, with an analysis row stating a *set* of rule ids checked in both directions so a fixture tripping a second unrelated rule fails rather than silently passing; every fixture in the new directory is listed, in both directions; and — the anti-vacuity gate — every corpus fixture actually reaches code generation unless a `STOPS_BEFORE_CODEGEN` allowlist names it with a reason, since a sweep asserting only "did not panic" over a program the type checker now rejects is looking at nothing. `Panicked` is a verdict the table exists to make declarable and that no row may declare ([#360])
- Test helper `CodegenAttempt` (`tests/src/utils.rs`) draws the distinction a `Result<_, String>` cannot: `Ok`, `Rejected` and `Panicked`. A backstop test has to assert that code generation *refused*, and a test checking only `is_err()` passes just as well when the compiler aborted — so every negative codegen test that exists to pin a diagnostic now matches on this instead. `codegen_attempt` also reports an analysis failure as `Rejected` rather than unwrapping it, which is what makes it usable on a rule-rejected program at all; the older `try_codegen` panicked the *test* on one. Six `void_forms` and five `unnamed_params` golden modules pin the newly accepted shapes as paired `.inf`/`.wasm`/`.wat` fixtures with a Wasmtime execution test each, including one that pins the export normalization bytes for `pub fn f(_: u8, b: i32)` and one that executes a bad enum tag through an unnamed parameter and observes the trap; a proof-mode test pins that `exists fn f(_: i32)` places its choice at slot 1, and reverting the arity fix alone turns it red. `negative.rs` gains `string_values` and `unit_values` asserting `Rejected` rather than merely `is_err()`, and two CLI integration tests check that a void `return;` builds with no `panicked` in stderr and that a `string` program fails with `error[A048]`, no `panicked`, and no artifact. A registry test pins that every rule id the code generation crate's diagnostics name exists in `all_rules()`, and reads the ids out of the sources with an anti-vacuity floor so a pattern that stopped matching fails instead of passing ([#360])
- `core/wasm-to-v/wasm-verifier-pin.txt` moves to `8f485f037a270271bf2e1393c24fc0684097163b`, the wasm-verifier revision whose companion proves `spec_overflow_realization.v` and whose manifest admits it as the seventh selected case: the artifact was published here first, proved there against these exact bytes, and pinned back, so the configured lane now requires the seven-case marker (`cases=7 proved=15 refuted=1`) and can pass it. No mirrored stub module changed, so no digest moves; every prose restatement of the revision moves with the pin ([#316])
- The selected-artifact discharge gate has a sixth case. `linked-extern` publishes `tests/test_data/rocq/spec_linked_extern.v`, and it is the only selected artifact whose raw bytes are not the output of one compilation: its producer compiles `spec_linked_extern.inf` and `spec_linked_extern_mathlib.inf` separately, merges them with `core/wasm-linker`, and translates the merged module — so `valid_spec_linked_extern__DoubleSpec` is the only certified endpoint about a body the compiler never emitted, applying `mathlib_double` at its post-merge `mod_funcs` ordinal. It is appended rather than ordered by shape because a case's position is the wire key its receipt is matched against. The aggregate floor moves from 5/11/1 to 6/13/1 everywhere it is stated: the request, the receipt validator, the success marker, the protected CI job, and the Docker adapter's ordered raw and receipt sets, whose five-way unrolled staging, fingerprint, copy and identity checks all gain the sixth entry. The `coqc` corpus gate and the discharge export now call one linked generator in `tests/src/rocq_test_support.rs`, so the bytes `coqc` elaborates and the bytes a companion proof is written against cannot come apart, and a `CaseSpec` states how its bytes are produced as one `Linkage` — single-file, or merged from named externals under a named policy — so "no externals but a policy" is unrepresentable and the externals stay under the same compile-time path checks the case's own names get. `core/wasm-to-v/wasm-verifier-pin.txt` moves to `fb0b2dd56bd451960197cf7e7ccdc513eea47d8b`, the wasm-verifier revision whose companion proves `spec_linked_extern.v` and whose manifest admits it: the artifact was published here first, proved there against these exact bytes, and pinned back, so the configured lane now requires the six-case marker and can pass it. Until then the protected `ci:real-rocq` lane fails closed on the six-case success marker and no six-case discharge success is claimed ([#362])
- Three new linked `coqc`-corpus fixtures cover the channels an obligation reaches a merged body through, which `spec_linked_extern.inf` alone does not. `spec_linked_exists.inf` and `spec_linked_unique.inf` put the merged ordinal inside a `reach_payload` — the only place the corpus pairs a reachability obligation with the merge that empties the import list `ValidExistsSpec`/`ValidUniqueSpec` need to construct a module runtime — and the two are not interchangeable, since the kinds land in different partitions under different predicates. `spec_linked_app_ok.inf` states `HA_app_ok` over a merged body, which is realization rather than application: a body that trapped would falsify it and leave the `T_app` claim standing. Each gets its own gate reading the ordinal off the module record, because every needle involved is already produced by a single-file fixture and a bare needle is not linked coverage. The `coqc` workflow's path filter is now `core/**`: enumerating crates had left `core/wasm-linker`, `core/hassert`, `core/fn-key` and `core/inference` outside it, so a change to any of the four could not trigger the gate that elaborates its output, and hosted Actions minutes are free on this public repository. `tools/inf-wasmparser/**` joins it as the only producer-chain crate outside `core/`, the one the translator and the linker read modules and name sections with; `tests/test_data/rocq/**` as the committed goldens the producer comparisons read; and `tests/src/rocq_test_support.rs` plus `tests/src/utils.rs` as the shared producer and what it builds every gated module through. The selection floor is raised to 45 — the `--list` total, which counts the `#[ignore]`d regeneration helpers — and the pass floor to 33, the gates that run; both are the counts the workflow enforces, measured rather than adjusted by arithmetic ([#362])
- Phase A of the emitted-Rocq dischargeability gate adds five ordered producer cases and a strict protocol without changing any existing generated bytes: bounded prime, exists, unique, narrow domain, and an explicit `assert(false)` negative certificate. Export freshly generates all five, requires byte identity with their committed goldens, publishes immutable raw/request hashes and exact 5/11/1 totals, rejects duplicate/unknown protocol data and stale or malformed receipts, probes the configured executable with a malformed same-basename input, and requires the false case's `ValidSpec` to be refuted rather than positively certified. The local path is Docker-only: pinned Rust 1.98 plus a tracked lane lock, locked fetch followed by offline builds, pinned BusyBox copying, `batch`/`single`/`both` adapter modes, exact container/provenance checks, private failure evidence, and optional `--full` coverage of the `inference-tests` crate (not the workspace). Phase A deliberately precedes the verifier proofs, manifest, inspector, and adapters in commit B, so its normal bridge path fails closed and no end-to-end discharge success is claimed until Inference commit C pins B ([#450])
- The Rocq gate is no longer stub-only. A new real-library lane type-checks every module the gate builds against the actual coq-wasm v2.2.0 and wasm-verifier libraries, reached through an `INFERENCE_WASM_VERIFIER_COQC` executable honouring an `<exe> <file.v>` contract, so the private library's checkout, build tree, Coq version and any container hop stay outside this repository and out of any public log. The lane runs a **provenance probe** before it believes a single verdict — a positive control naming `HA_pto` and `ktrue` (declared upstream, deliberately absent from the stub) and a negative control naming a constructor no library declares — which is what stops an oracle that merely exits zero from reporting every module green. Measured: an oracle of `/usr/bin/true` is rejected on the negative control, one that always fails is rejected on the positive control, and **the vendored stub pointed at the lane is rejected on the positive control**, so the lane cannot be quietly satisfied by the very stub it exists to check. Absent an oracle the lane skips with a message naming what the run did not establish, rather than reporting a bare pass ([#359])
- A new stub-drift check holds the vendored signature stub to the upstream libraries at pinned revisions, read with `git show` so a checkout parked elsewhere cannot change the verdict. It runs in two directions, because a one-directional check is a false green: the *fiction* direction requires every name and every constructor shape the stub declares to exist upstream and agree — this is the [#230]/[#346] arity class — and the *narrowing* direction digests the set of declarations upstream has and the stub does not, so upstream growing or dropping one is a change somebody is asked about. The coq-wasm tier needs no credential and runs in CI; the wasm-verifier tier runs where a checkout exists and skips loudly otherwise. It found the stub declaring `module_glob` where upstream declares `module_global`, and a `Notation seq := list` that in the context an emitted module's preamble builds resolves to `Coq.Lists.List.seq : nat -> nat -> list nat` rather than to `list` — both now fixed ([#359])
- The Rocq CI job can no longer pass having run nothing. `cargo test <filter>` exits 0 when the filter selects nothing, and the job was a single filtered invocation, so a renamed module, a dropped `mod` line in `tests/src/lib.rs`, or a one-character typo in the filter left it permanently green with no Rocq elaborated at all — measured, `cargo test -p inference-tests "rocq_typecheck_nope::"` exits 0. The job now asserts a floor on the tests the filter *selects* and a second floor on the tests that actually *pass*, the latter closing the case a selection floor cannot see: a full selection that runs nothing because every gate was ignored. `tests/src/lib.rs` joins both path allowlists, since dropping a `mod` line there deletes a whole gate and a filename allowlist would not have noticed ([#359])

- The linker envelope's acceptance criterion is a checked-in fixture: `tests/test_data/wasmlib/rustlib.wasm`, the unmodified output of `cargo build --release --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` over the `#![no_std]` crate committed beside it. Every other linker fixture is written in WAT or Inference source, so none could answer whether a *foreign* compiler's output fits. `extern_link_toolchain.rs` merges it into `infc`-produced mains and asserts computed results under `wasmtime` — a Tier-A leaf saturating at `2147483647`, and a Tier-B loop summing an Inference `[i32; 4]` local through the pointer it is handed, the Rust body and the Inference frame agreeing on where the bytes are — plus the `TierBInMultiPageMemory` warning naming the sixteen pages a memoryless main adopts, and the artifact's own premise, which nothing else states: a `producers` section naming `rustc`, the `__stack_pointer` global that makes classifying globals on use rather than declaration load-bearing, and the absence of the data segment, element segment and table that would make it Tier C. The third export, `mulhi`, gives the width-changing conversions their first real-toolchain witness: writing a computation over `i64` is not enough to keep them, since an optimizer narrows any intermediate it can reach more cheaply — `clamp_add` is written that way and arrives as branchless `i32` — so the fixture carries a high-product, whose 64-bit intermediate no 32-bit lowering computes. The `coqc` corpus gains `spec_linked_toolchain.inf`, whose three obligations apply all three merged bodies, so a `BI_select` standing in for a branch LLVM removed, a `BI_loop` carrying a result type, and `BI_cvtop` in both directions — none with an emitter arm in this compiler — are elaborated rather than merely permitted. The negative-constant scan now covers linked modules, this being the first corpus module whose constants a fixture author did not choose, and the `coqc` workflow's path filter gained `tests/test_data/wasmlib/**`. See `tests/test_data/wasmlib/README.md` for the toolchain, the regeneration recipe, and why an unoptimized build of the same crate falls outside the envelope ([#363])
- The `coqc` gate accepts **linked** fixtures: a corpus entry may name external `.inf` modules, which are compiled on their own, statically merged into the main fixture, and translated post-merge — the pipeline a real `infc -L` build runs, where before the gate only ever compiled one file and handed the raw codegen bytes to `wasm_to_v`. `spec_linked_extern.inf` is the first such entry and the only producer of a `T_app` whose target is a body the compiler never emitted; its obligation was discharged for real against wasm-verifier (both `ValidModule` and `ValidSpec` closed with `Qed`, no `Admitted`/`admit`/`Abort`, and `Print Assumptions` within that repo's existing allowlist). Every single-file entry keeps its current path and output ([#363])
- Three new `coqc`-gate corpus fixtures cover the aggregate work end to end — `spec_aggregate_values.inf` (leaf encoding, enumeration order across ranks and struct field widths, literals, copies, leafwise comparison, existential leaves), `spec_bounded_iteration.inf` (the symbolic-index witness, a constant step descending before a symbolic one, a field step, and both the signed and unsigned index spellings) and `spec_quantifier_alternation.inf` (∃∀ nesting, with `"Hall "` added to `REQUIRED_CONSTRUCTS` as a needle the alternation fixture uniquely produces). Every obligation of all three was additionally proved `Qed` against real wasm-verifier before the goldens were committed, which is how the bounded-iteration fixture's first draft was caught: it emitted a *false* obligation — an index constrained only from above, refutable at `i = -1` — and was rewritten rather than shipped as the documented example of the encoding ([#355])
- The `coqc` gate now also audits, mechanically, that every constructor the vendored stub declares is elaborated by some module the gate compiles, closing what a hand-maintained needle list alone could not catch — the exact gap the [#230] `BI_forall` arity bug shipped through. New fixtures take obligation-printer operator coverage from 3 of 23 arms reachable at both `i32` and `i64` to full coverage, plus WASM module/instruction and `hassert`-printer surfaces no Inference source reaches. Seven stub declarations the emitter can never produce were deleted rather than exempted (see `rocq-stub/README.md`). This proves the stub is fully exercised, not that it still matches wasm-verifier's real `Assertions.v` ([#359]) ([#401])
- `infs` unit tests that write an executable stub and spawn it no longer fail intermittently with `ETXTBSY`; a shared `retry_while_exec_busy` helper retries spawns racing fork-inherited write descriptors ([#345])
- Tests outside `apps/infs` no longer race on fixed temporary paths — the class [#331] closed inside `apps/infs` and left standing elsewhere; every scratch path now comes from a `tempfile::TempDir`, fixing two live defects in codegen and cross-compiler tests ([#343])
- `infs` unit tests no longer collide on temporary directories across concurrent test processes; scratch dirs now come from `assert_fs::TempDir`, replacing constant paths and cross-process-colliding `fastrand` suffixes ([#331])
- `infs` unit tests no longer write the process-global `INFS_VERBOSE`; the predicate moved into a pure `verbose_from(Option<&OsStr>)` and `read_infs_metadata_with_verbosity`, removing an unsynchronized env read/write race ([#331])
- New `coqc`-gated fixture `tests/test_data/inf/spec_negative_consts.inf` carries negative constants through every constant-emitting position, and `negative_constants_are_parenthesized` scans the whole corpus for the unparenthesized spelling ([#314])
- Bump the test suite's `wasmtime` execution-harness dependency from 43.0.0 to 47.0.3, picking up the fix for [RUSTSEC-2026-0222](https://rustsec.org/advisories/RUSTSEC-2026-0222.html); no compiler output changes ([#335])
